# Live streamer commerce: exploring how the interconnections among brand, streamer, and follower translate into behavioural outcomes

1

Live Streamer Commerce: Exploring how the interconnections
among brand, streamer, and follower translate into
behavioural outcomes

Mengqi Wu

A thesis submitted for the degree of

Doctor of Philosophy
                                                                  in
Management

Essex Business School
University of Essex

12/2025

2

Abstract
This study investigates the psychological and behavioural mechanisms that
drive consumer engagement and impulse buying in live streaming commerce.
As an emerging form of digital marketing, live streaming integrates real -time
interaction, sense of presence , and brand messaging to create immersive
consumer experiences. Drawing upon a multi -theoretical framework —
comprising Source Credibility Theory, Congruity Theory, Flow Theory, and
Consumer Engagement Theory —this research explores how streamer
attributes (attractiveness, expertise, trustworthiness), brand–streamer–follower
congruence, and the sense of presence contribute to dynamic brand
experiences, which in turn influence follower engagement and impulse buying
behaviour.

To address these aims, a quantitative research design was employed using
structured online questionnaires conducted in both China and the UK, enabling
cross-cultural analysis of follower behaviours. Data collected from 600
participants (300 per country) were analysed using Structural Equation
Modelling (SEM) using SmartPLS to test direct, mediating, and moderating
relationships among the constructs.

The findings confirm that streamer attributes positively influence dynamic brand
experience, with sense of presence and congruence acting as critical
psychological mechanisms. Sense of presence showed a positive mediating
effect among Chinese followers but not a very positive effect in the UK sample,
highlighting important cross-cultural differences. The congruence emerged as a

3

consistently strong predictor of dynamic brand experience across both markets.
Dynamic brand experience, in turn, strongly drives follower engagement and
impulse buying, with gender and product type not moderating the strength of
these effects in engagement and impulse buying . The study contributes to
theoretical advancement by extending the application of existing models to real-
time, interactive commerce environments and offers actionable insights for
marketers, streamers, and brands seeking to enhance the effectiveness of live-
streaming strategies.

Keywords: Live Streaming Commerce, Digital Marketing, Consumer
Behaviour, Branding, Sense of Presence

4

1 Chapter 1: Introduction

This chapter serves as the introduction to the live streaming commerce research study,
providing an overview of the key elements that frame the investigation of live streaming
commerce research. This chapter begins by establishing the research background and
rationale, and the research problem is clearly explained. The research objectives and
questions are then presented to guide the study.

This chapter also discusses the chosen research methods, including the adoption of
a quantitative methods approach to gather comprehensive insights. Furthermore, the
potential contributions of the study are outlined, encompassing theoretical, managerial,
methodological, and contextual dimensions. The chapter acknowledges the limitations
and delimitations of the research, ensuring a transparent understanding of its scope
and potential constraints. Finally, the structure of the thesis is outlined, providing
readers with a roadmap for the subsequent chapters.

Overall, Chapter 1 sets the stage for the live  streaming research, contextualising the
topic and outlining the significance and objectives of the investigation.

15

1.1 What is Live Streaming Commerce
Live streaming commerce is a form of digital retail where products are promoted and
sold in real time through interactive livestreams hosted by streamers, influencers, or
brands. Unlike traditional e -commerce, where consumers passively browse static
listings, live streaming commerce integrates real -time video, interactivity, and sales
promotion into a single platform. Streamers showcase products, answer questions,
and respond instantly to audience comments, creating a sense of presence,
authenticity, and urgency that can drive immediately following and purchase decisions
(Zhang et al., 2022; Luo et al., 2023).

For example, Figure 1 shows below, in the UK, The Body Shop uses TikTok Shop
livestreams to promote skincare and beauty products, offering exclusive discounts and
flash sales during the broadcast. Similarly, in China, L’Oréal Paris regularly hosts
livestreams on Taobao Live, where professional hosts demonstrate product benefits,
interact with viewers, and provide limited-time coupons and gifts to encourage impulse
purchases.

16

Figure 1:Live Streaming Commerce

These examples illustrate how live streaming commerce operates at the intersection
of entertainment, interactivity, and retail. Streamers act not only as presenters but also
as sales agents and relationship builders, using personal credibility and audience
engagement to influence consumer decision -making. The popularity of this practice
has led to its rapid expansion in both Western markets, where it is emerging, and Asian
markets, such as China, where it is already highly developed.

1.2 Research Background
Live streaming commerce (LSC) has its roots in the home shopping television
programmes that emerged in the 1990s (McDonald, 1995). Similar to channels such
as QVC (Quality, Value, Convenience television shopping channel) and HSN (Home

17

Shopping Network  television channel ), these programmes featured live product
demonstrations, persuasive sales dialogues, and opportunities for viewers to make
purchases while watching TV (Park et al., 2011; Yen, 2018) . However, unlike
traditional TV shopping, which relied on one -way communication via television
networks, LSC leverages social media and digital platforms to enable real -time, two-
way interaction between streamers and viewers, as well as engagement among
viewers themselves. This interactive capacity, supported by integrated e -commerce
functions, represents a fundamental shift from the more passive, broadcast-based TV
shopping format to a socially driven, participatory shopping experience.
Given the global expansion of live streaming commerce, this study focuses on China
and the UK as two important yet contrasting markets. China represents the world’s
most mature live streaming commerce ecosystem, while the UK is one of the fastest -
growing Western markets. Comparing these two countries provides valuable insights
into how cultural and market differences may influence consumer responses to live
streaming commerce.  In another aspect, w ith the rapid advancement of digital
technologies and the widespread adoption of the internet, social media platforms have
proliferated, giving rise to new forms of online interaction and commerce. Among these,
platforms that integrate live streaming and e -commerce functionalities have gained
significant popularity, enabling users to engage with their favourite streamers in real
time. Through various platforms such as Douyin, TikTok, RedNote, and Instagram Live,
viewers could join sessions based on interest  or product category, interact with
streamers, and experience a sense of social presence and immersion during the
process (Wongkitrungrueng & Assarut, 2020; Ma, 2021).

18

Since 2020, the growth of live streaming platforms has accelerated in both China and
the UK ( Mintel, 2021; Statista, 2021). According to Apple Store rankings, TikTok was
the most downloaded app globally in 2024 (Statista, 2025) , indicating its dominant
position in the digital landscape. In China, platforms such as Douyin and Red Notes,
both of which support live streaming functions, also achieved top download positions,
reflecting strong consumer interest and acceptance of this interactive format.
Furthermore, data from  Statista, (2025)  shows a consistent upward trend in the
number of live streaming viewers in both China (Appendix 5)
and the UK Live Streaming Market  growth rate is expected to be around 25.65%
(Marketresearch, 2025) , underscoring live -streaming’s rising influence on follower
engagement and impulse buying behaviour.

Also, live streaming has emerged as a compelling tool for marketers due to its ability
to deliver real -time interaction, product demonstrations, and personal engagement,
which can influence impulse buying and foster brand loyalty (Sun et al., 2019,Hugh et
al., 2022) . Viewers can ask questions, receive instant feedback, and observe
streamers dynamically adapt content based on context. These features create a rich
media environment that surpasses traditional marketing formats, enhancing emotional
connection and authent icity (Yu & Zheng, 2021) . Additionally, elements such as
lighting, music, visuals, and streamer charisma can evoke a strong sense of presence,
making followers feel "present" in the virtual shopping space (Ming et al., 2021; Zheng;
Li, Dou & Yu, 2023).

However, despite these advantages, live streaming is not without its challenges and
risks. The live and unscripted nature of the format can lead to unpredictable

19

outcomes—including technical failures, misinformation, or controversial content—that
can damage a brand’s reputation (Li & Lee, 2024; Ding et al., 2025; Khan et al., 2025).
Streamers may also face pressure to maintain constant enthusiasm or high sales
performance, leading to burnout or ethical compromises. From the consumer side,
over-commercialised or insincere content may reduce trust and trigger scepticism,
particularly if the authenticity of the streamer is questioned  (Kanaveedu &
Kalapurackal, 2022; Bu et al., 2022). Additionally, not all campaigns achieve their
intended ROI, and some live streaming efforts fall short in generating meaningful
engagement or conversions (Manning & Muzellec, 2021).

Given this context, a key challenge and opportunity for marketers is understanding
how to attract and engage followers through live streaming and how this real -time
interaction could drive unconscious or impulse purchasing behaviours. As live
streaming con tinues to evolve into a powerful and immersive marketing tool, it
presents a valuable and timely area for academic investigation, particularly in
exploring the psychological, relational, and experiential mechanisms that shape
follower engagement in this dy namic environment. In the age of digital influence and
real-time engagement, live streaming commerce has taken advantage of the power of
live streaming as a potent marketing tool, providing a unique platform to immediately
and interactively connect with th eir followers. As defined by Wongkitrungrueng,
Dehouche & Assarut (2020) Live streaming commerce is a form of digital retail where
products are promoted and sold in real -time through interactive video streamer,
combining entertainment, follower engagement, and instant purchasing capabilities .
As this dynamic landscape continues to evolve, delving into the interaction of digital
marketing through streamers and its impact on follower engagement and  impulse

20

buying emerges as a compelling area of research (Luo et al., 2023; Aggarwal & and
Shah, 2024; Cheah, Koay & Lim, 2024).

By contrast, live streaming commerce has many advantages over traditional marketing.
Firstly, during live streaming, the streamer could show products in real -time through
live streaming platforms. Also, the streamer could answer the followers' questions in
the chat box or have a voice chat w ith followers through the live streaming platform
(Wang & Wu, 2019) . Secondly, streamers' live streaming content is dynamic, not
limited by region or presentation format; it could be text, voice, or any display that
could, according to different situations, also could make timely adjustments which
providing richer information than static text or images (Wongkitrungrueng & Assarut,
2020; Ma, 2021). Thirdly, the live streaming background music, lights and pictures of
streamers could be adjusted according to different scenes and products (Yu & Zheng,
2021). Fourthly, streamers could bring a sense of presence to their followers (Sun et
al., 2019). The sense of presence in live streaming commerce refers to the degree to
which streamers immerse themselves in and interact with the digital environment,
creating a feeling of "being there" for the audience and fostering deeper connections
and engagement (Ming et al., 2021; Zheng, Wu & Liao, 2023; Li, Dou & Yu, 2023) .
This means that the followers’ interaction with streamers through the live streaming
platform could reduce their perception of uncertainty and increase followers’
perception of authenticity (Sun et al., 2019).

1.2.1 Theoretical Perspective
Theoretically, this research is grounded in Source Credibility Theory, Congruity Theory,
and Flow Theory, which offer insights into how consumers perceive streamers in terms

21

of credibility, expertise, authenticity, and congruence. While these models are well -
established, their combined application in real -time live streaming environments
remains underdeveloped.  Combining these theories allows a more holistic
understanding of live streaming commerce. Source Credibility Theory explains
streamer characteristics, Congruity Theory explains perceived alignment, Flow Theory
captures immersive experiences, and Customer E ngagement Theory explains
behavioural outcomes. Together, they provide a comprehensive framework for
examining follower engagement and impulse buying.  For example, while physical
attractiveness is commonly cited as a signal of credibility, recent findings suggest that
its influence on engagement and impulse buying may be context-dependent or weaker
than expected (Li, Feng & Zhao, 2023; Li et al., 2025), indicating that the relationship
remains theoretically and empirically contested. Similarly, congruence—the alignment
between streamer, brand, and audience values—has emerged as a powerful predictor
of consumer trust, credibility, and purchase intentions. Prior studies (Liu, Zhang &
Zhang, 2020) highlight its importance in shaping consumer attitudes, yet its role within
real-time, interactive live streaming commerce remains insufficiently understood. In
particular, little is known about how congruence operates dynamically during live
interactions to influence follower engagement and impulse buying.

1.2.2 Practitioner’s  Perspective
From the practitioner’s perspective, many different types of businesses have started
to consider live streaming as an important part of their digital marketing strategy
(Zhang et al., 202 2; Luo et al., 2023) , for example, fashion businesses, food
businesses, some technology product companies, etc. For instance, fashion brands
like Louis Vuitton and Shein have hosted live runway shows and product launches

22

(Global Cosmetics Media, 2026); food companies such as Haidilao in China have used
live streaming for kitchen tours and promotions; and tech brands like Xiaomi and
Samsung have employed live product announcements and interactive Q&A sessions
to engage audiences(South China Morning Post, 2020).

Businesses with streamers could create authentic and relevant content through live
streaming platforms that resonate with the target audience. By leveraging the trust and
connection that streamers build with their followers, products could effectively reac h
and engage with the customer base they want  (Wei & Xi, 2023; Chen, Pham & Tran,
2024). For example, in China, Li Jiaqi (known as the “Lipstick King”) has successfully
promoted cosmetic brands like L’Oréal and Perfect Diary through his trusted influencer
persona. Similarly, in the UK, influencers on TikTok Shop such as Abby Roberts have
collaborated with beauty and fashion brands to drive sales through authentic, real-time
interactions. Yet, strategic alignment alone is not enough; a deeper understanding of
the mechanisms behind consumer engagement is essential to harness the full
potential of live streaming commerce.

Strategic alignment alone is not enough; a deeper understanding of the mechanisms
behind consumer engagement is essential to harness the full potential of live
streaming commerce. This is because engagement in live streaming environments is
shaped by dynami c and psychologically rich interactions that go beyond brand -fit or
product relevance. Factors such as the perceived authenticity of the streamer, the
sense of real -time presence, emotional resonance, and the interactive flow of
communication all contribut e to follower attention, trust, and eventual purchase
behaviour (Brodie et al., 2011; Lim et al., 2022). Without unpacking how these

23

experiential elements function in tandem, businesses may struggle to convert viewers
into active consumers, even with strategically aligned streamers or content.

1.2.3 Industry Perspective
From the live streaming industry perspective, with market forecasts suggesting the live
streaming commerce sector's potential to double annually, this medium presents vast
growth opportunities for digital marketing practitioners. (Statista, 2022 ). In the
landscape of live streaming, the consistent yearly rise in live streaming users by tens
of millions is a testament to its growing influence  (Appendix 5) (Statisa, 2024). live
streaming commerce, in particular, has rapidly emerged as a central strategy, creating
a unique and captivating platform for streamers to engage with their followers. These
real-time interactions, characterised by their immediacy and authenticity, enhance
audience participation and establish a deeper sense of connection (Hugh et al., 2022).

1.2.4 Streamers’ Perspective
From the streamers’ perspective, live streaming's advantage is the unrestricted
environment it creates, enabling streamers to directly converse, engage, and resonate
with their followers (Wies, Bleier & Edeling, 2022) . Platforms like Instagram Live,
Facebook Live, and TikTok Live have become popular for these real-time discussions,
Q&A sessions, and interactions, providing deeper connections between streamers
and their audiences (Sun et al., 2019) . Besides substantiating the efficacy of this
approach, live streaming campaigns have consistently demonstrated their influence in
enhancing product awareness, brand image and consumers' purchase intentions
(Abell & Biswas, 2023).  Recognising these benefits, brands are strategically aligning
with streamers whose audience mirrors their desired target market (Wies, Bleier &

24

Edeling, 2022) . Such collaborations are importantl, especially when research
highlights consumers' proclivity to perceive streamers as reliable sources and
endorsements (Barta et al., 2023) . So, streamers play an important role in follower
engagement.

In the realm of live streaming commerce, the interplay among dynamic brand
experiences, sense of presence, and brand –streamer–follower congruence —
particularly in their effects on engagement and impulse buying —remains largely
unexplored. For instance, Sun et al.,( 2019);  caution that “few studies have
investigated how live ‑streaming enhances customer engagement,” underscoring the
need for deeper inquiry. Similarly, Addo, Fang, and Asare (2021) observe that while
customer engagement significantly predicts purchase intentions in live -streaming
platforms, there is still a lack of comprehensive models outlining how these processes
unfold. Although impulse buying is recognised as a common outcome of live
commerce, empirical studies like Huang and Suo (2021) largely focus on promotional
or visual triggers—such as price promotions and visual appeal—rather than examining
the mediating psychological mechanisms behind consumer reactions

Together, these sources reveal a striking limitation: most existing studies concentrate
on isolated stimuli or outcomes, leaving the underlying cognitive and affective
mechanisms—such as immersion, authenticity, and congruence —that drive
engagement and imp ulsive behaviour insufficiently theorised.  This avenue of
interaction holds the potential to reshape the understanding of the streamer brand and
followers (Manning & Muzellec, 2021) . Also, as viewers observe streamers actively
engaging with comments, answering queries, and participating in authentic

25

conversations during live streams, the perception of their credibility and expertise
could be influenced (Hugh et al., 2022) .  Therefore, this study focuses on exploring
how streamer attributes, sense of presence, and brand –streamer–follower
congruence influence dynamic brand experiences, follower engagement, and impulse
buying. By acknowledging both the potential of live streaming commerce, the research
aims to provide a more balanced, theoretically grounded, and practically relevant
understanding of this fast-evolving digital phenomenon.

1.3 Research Gaps
Live streaming commerce is undergoing a transformation with the advent of live
streaming platforms.These live streaming platforms have special  characterise like
real-time interaction, streamers could influence followers’ behaviour online (Wang &
Wu, 2019; Wongkitrungrueng, Dehouche & Assarut, 2020). While these live streaming
commerce are beginning to be explored for their potential impact on consumer
behaviour, comprehensive academic investigations into these areas remain limited
(Leung, Gu & Palmatier, 2022; Bu, Parkinson & Thaichon, 2022; Hugh et al., 2022) .
For instance, Leung, Gu, and Palmatier (2022) note that “research on live streaming
commerce is still in its infancy, with many unanswered questions about consumer –
seller interactions and engagement outcomes.” Similarly, Bu, Parkinson, and
Thaichon (2022) argue that although live streaming commerce has emerged as a
disruptive force, “systematic academic inquiry into its drivers and consequences
remains limited compared to its rapid commercial adoption.” Hugh et al. (2022) also
emphasise that “the theoretical understanding of consumer behaviour in live streaming
contexts is fragmented, calling for integrative models that capture the interplay of trust,
presence, and persuasion.”  By analysi s and review of existing academic literature

26

indicate that the current field of live streaming commerce is divided into 5 main
directions:

(1) Followers’ buying behaviour via streamers’ physical attraction (Sun et al., 201 9;
Clement Addo et al., 2021; Zhu et al., 2022; Pink, Wilkie & Graves, 2023); This stream
of research primarily examines how physical attractiveness and perceived appeal of
streamers act as persuasive cues that drive viewers’ purchase intentions and impulse
buying.

(2) Follower engagement on live streaming platforms (Jin, Muqaddam & Ryu, 2019;
Belanche et al., 2021; Barta et al., 2023) ; This category focuses on how interactivity,
parasocial relationships, and real -time communication foster sustained audience
involvement, loyalty, and active participation during live streams.

(3) Brand experience impacts streamer and their followers (Lou & Yuan, 2019;
Wongkitrungrueng & Assarut, 202 0; Kim & Kim, 2021; Crnjak -Karanović, Kursan
Milaković & Elez, 202 3). This research area explores how multi -sensory, immersive,
and interactive experiences created in live streaming environments shape both brand
perceptions and consumer behavioural outcomes, such as trust, engagement, and
purchase behaviour.

(4) Contextual moderators and boundary conditions in live streaming commerce (e.g.,
gender, product type(Abosag et al., 2020; Rollin, 2022; Arora et al., 2023): this stream
examines how individual differences and situational/contextual factors shape the

27

strength and direction of effects from streamer cues and live streaming experiences
to consumer behaviours such as engagement and impulse buying.

(5) Cross-cultural perspectives in live streaming commerce: research comparing how
cultural contexts (e.g., China vs. the UK) shape the effects of streamer attributes,
presence, and brand experiences is still limited, despite evidence that cultural values
influence consumer engagement and consumer behaviour (Jeong & Lee, 2019; Wang
et al., 2021).

Although those 5 areas above are growing in significance in both academia and live
streaming commerce practice, the literature remains fragmented, and there are some
potential problems worth exploring.  For example, Prior studies have tended to focus
separately on streamers’ appeal (Sun et al., 2019; Clement Addo et al., 2021), follower
engagement (Jin, Muqaddam & Ryu, 2019; Barta et al., 2023) , or dynamic brand
experiences (Lou & Yuan, 2019; Wongkitrungrueng & Assarut, 2020; Kim & Kim, 2021)
without integrating these strands into a unified framework that reflects the complexity
of live streaming environments. So, this study fills these gaps.

First: Although existing studies have examined individual streamer attributes such as
expertise, trustworthiness, and attractiveness, few have explored how these attributes
influence the sense of presence and Congruence then bring an immersive experience
(Lou & Yuan2019 , Li & Xie 2020 ). Additionally, the interplay between streamers’
attributes, sense of presence and congruence in real -time live streaming settings
remains underexplored. So this study addresses this gap by investigating how

28

streamers’ attributes impact the sense of presence and congruence in a live streaming
commerce context.

Second: while prior research has explored sense of presence in digital environments,
there is limited understanding of how presence , particularly social presence and
telepresence, interacts with dynamic brand experience  to influence follower
engagement and impulse buying in live streaming contexts  (Ye, Zheng & Li, 2022;
Sang et al., 2023) . Existing studies rarely examine the sense of presence as a
mediating psychological mechanism within a real-time, interactive commerce setting.
So, this study addresses this gap by investigating how the sense of presence impacts
dynamic brand experiences and indirectly impacts engagement and impulse buying.

Third: Although prior studies have acknowledged the importance of congruence
between brands, streamers, and followers in shaping consumer behaviour, most
research has focused on static or one -dimensional relationships, particularly within
traditional influencer m arketing contexts (Deng, Chen & Wu, 2024; Opić  & Pandža
Bajs, 2024; Roth-Cohen, Segev & Liu, 2024). Existing literature has yet to fully explore
how streamer–follower congruence operates dynamically in real -time live streaming
environments, where immediacy, interactivity, and authenticity continuously  shape
perceptions of alignment. Moreover, little is known about how streamer –follower
congruence functions as a mediating mechanism linking streamer attributes (e.g.,
credibility, expertise) to dynamic brand experiences and engagement and impulse
buying. Addressing this gap, the current study investigates congruence as an evolving,
interactive construct that plays an important role in enhancing brand experiences and
influencing follower behaviours within live streaming commerce.

29

Fourth: While existing studies have examined the influence of streamer attributes on
consumer perceptions and behaviours (Hugh et al. 2022) , limited research has
explored the role of dynamic brand experiences  within this relationship, especially in
live streaming commerce contexts. Prior literature often treats brand experience as a
direct outcome, but rarely as a psychological mechanism that connects credible
streamer characteristics with follower engagement and impulse buying  (Ye, Zheng &
Li, 2022; Sang et al., 2023) . This gap is particularly obvious in real -time, interactive
environments where consumers’ immersive experiences shape  follower engagement
and impulse buying  (Liao et al., 2022; Li, Feng & Zhao, 2023; Khoi & Le, 2025) .
Addressing this gap, this study investigates how dynamic brand experiences act as a
mediator between streamer attributes and follower engagement and impulse buying ,
contributing new insights to both theory and practice.

Fifith: Although previous studies have examined individual elements such as streamer
credibility, authenticity, or attractiveness, and their influence on engagement or
impulse buying, they have typically done so in isolation rather than through an
integrated theoretical lens. Existing research has primarily focused on surface -level
engagement metrics or single -factor analyses, overlooking the complex interplay
between streamer attributes, brand dynamics, and follower responses
(Wongkitrungrueng, Dehouche & Assarut, 2020; Wymer et al., 2021; Meisner &
Ledbetter, 2022; Zheng, Li & Na, 2022). Therefore, a comprehensive model that unites
the S-O-R framework, Source Credibility Theory, and Congruence Theory is needed
to capture how these interconnected factors collectively influence dynamic brand
experience, follower engagement, and impulse buy ing, while considering moderating
effects such as gender and product type within live streaming commerce.

30

All in all, the study aims to extend and integrate multiple established frameworks —
Source Credibility Theory, Congruity Theory, Flow Theory, and Consumer
Engagement Theory —within the emerging and under -theorised domain of live
streaming commerce. Although these theories have been individually applied to
various areas of digital marketing, their combined application in a real-time, interactive
commerce environment remains limited (Wongkitrungrueng & Assarut, 2020a; Ming
et al., 2021; Ma, 2021b; Barta, Gurrea & Flavián, 2023). By investigating how streamer
attributes, sense of presence, and brand –streamer–follower congruence influences
dynamic brand experiences, and indirectly influence follower engagement and impulse
buying. The study introduces a more  comprehensive and psychologically grounded
framework for understanding follower engagement and impulse buying  behaviour in
live streaming environments.

1.4 Research Scope and Delimitations
This study is positioned within the discipline between marketing  and psychology,
specifically consumer psychology, with strong overlaps in social and cognitive
psychology. The research focuses on the psychological mechanisms that explain how
consumers perceive, process, and respond to live streaming commerce environments.
In particular, the study emphasises cognitive and affective processes —such as
credibility judgements, presence, congruence, and experiential immersion —and their
engagement and impulse buying  behaviour. By positioning the research in this way,
the aim is to develop a theoretically grounded model that advances understanding of
live streaming commerce from a psychological rather than a sociological or cultural
lens.

31

While sociocultural and contextual dimensions are recognised as important influences,
an in-depth exploration of these factors is beyond the scope of this study. For example,
cultural norms, platform-specific affordances, or economic structures may shape how
live streaming commerce develops, but these are not the central focus here. Instead,
cultural context is addressed through cross -country sampling (China and the UK) to
test the robustness of the psychological model across two distinct markets, rather than
to conduct a sociocultural comparison. This delimitation ensures that the study
remains conceptually coherent and focused on psychological processes, rather than
extending into broader cultural, sociological, or economic inquiries.
The scope is also delimited to live streaming commerce, where products are promoted
in real time by streamers through interactive sessions. Other forms of digital marketing,
such as static posts, stories, or pre -recorded videos, are excluded from this study.
Similarly, platform -specific technical features (e.g., TikTok algorithms, Taobao’s
payment integration) are not explored in depth, as the focus is on psycholog ical and
experiential mechanisms that can be generalised across platforms.
Finally, the study is delimited to a quantitative approach using online  questionnaire.
Qualitative methods such as interviews or ethnography are not included, as the
primary objective is to statistically test the proposed model and its relationships. The
temporal scope is also limited, acknowledging that live streaming commerce evolves
rapidly; thus, findings represent a snapshot rather than a longitudinal account of this
phenomenon.
By establishing these boundaries, the research maintains a clear psychological
orientation, offering a focused and rigorous investigation of follower engagement and

32

impulse buying  behaviour in live streaming commerce while recognising, but not
directly addressing, broader cultural and contextual dimensions.

1.5 Research Objectives
In response to the growing relevance of live streaming commerce and digital marketing
and its ability to influence follower engagement and impulse buying behaviour in real
time, this study aims to explore the psychological and experiential mechanisms that
drive follower engagement and impulse buying. Specifically, the research focuses on
how various elements —such as streamer attributes, sense of presence, brand -
streamer-follower congruence, and dynamic brand experiences —interact to shape
consumer responses within live streaming environments. The study also considers
individual differences and contextual factors, such as gender and product type, which
may moderate these effects. The research is guided by the following objectives:

Objective 1:  To explore how streamers’ attributes, brand –streamer–follower
congruence, and the immersive nature of live -streaming environments
collectively shape dynamic brand experiences and influence follower
engagement and impulse buying behaviour.

This objective seeks to understand how multiple antecedents —such as a streamer’s
expertise, trustworthiness, and attractiveness, combined with congruence and
immersive interactivity —jointly influence brand experiences. By examining these
factors together, the research aims to contribute to a comprehensive understanding of
how live streaming creates emotional, cognitive, and behavioural responses. This is

33

important because prior literature has often studied these elements in isolation (Chen
& Yang 2023;  Zheng et al.2023) , leaving gaps in how they operate in combination
within a real-time interactive environment.

Objective 2: To examine the sense of presence and brand –streamer–follower
congruence impact the relationship between streamers’ attributes and dynamic
brand experiences, and how these experiences drive follower engagement and
impulse buying behaviour outcomes.

Although the sense of presence and congruence are acknowledged as important in
digital and social commerce, their specific mediating roles in live streaming remain
underexplored (He, Li & Xue, 2022; Li & Xie, 2020) . This objective aims to clarify the
psychological processes through which credibility-based streamer attributes translate
into consumer engagement and impulse buying. Addressing this provides theoretical
value by advancing the understanding of how and why streamers influence consumer
decisions beyond surface-level cues.

Objective 3: To investigate the effects of gender and product type on the
relationship between dynamic brand experiences and follower engagement and
impulse buying behaviour.

While dynamic brand experiences have been shown to affect consumer behaviour,
these effects are unlikely to be uniform across all consumers or contexts. For example,
low versus high product prices, fashion versus technology products, or gender-based
preferences may alter how strongly brand experiences translate into engagement and

34

purchases (Khoi, Le & Nguyen Dong, 2023; Wei & Xi, 2023; Li, Xuan & Chen, 2024) .
This objective addresses the need for more precise insights into heterogeneity in
follower engagement and impulse buying behaviour responses, helping to explain the
boundary conditions of live streaming commerce effectiveness.

Objective 4: To provide practical insights for brands, streamers, and marketers
on how to design more effective live -streaming strategies by leveraging
psychological mechanisms that enhance consumer engagement and purchase
intent.

Beyond theoretical contributions, this study has strong applied relevance. By
identifying which streamer attributes and psychological mechanisms are most
effective, and under what conditions, the findings will guide practitioners in optimising
streamer selection, campaign design, and platform strategy. This objective ensures
that the research outcomes extend beyond academic knowledge to provide tangible
value for industry stakeholders seeking to maximise the return on investment in live
streaming commerce.

1.6 Research Question
Building upon the objectives outlined in the previous section, this study is guided by a
series of research questions designed to explore the mechanisms through which live
streaming commerce influences follower engagement and impulse buying. The
questions aim to uncover the direct and indirect effects of key variables such as
streamer attributes, sense of presence, congruence, and dynamic brand experience,
as well as to identify moderating influences like gender and product type. These

35

questions are central to addressing the research problem and provide the foundation
for the study’s theoretical model and empirical analysis.
The research questions are as follows:

Research Question 1: How do streamers’ attributes and the immersive nature of
the live -streaming environment influence dynamic brand experiences and
follower engagement and impulse buying behaviour outcomes?

This question responds directly to calls in the literature for a more integrated
understanding of streamer attributes and their effects. While prior research has
examined credibility factors (e.g., trustworthiness, attractiveness, expertise) or
congruence in isolation, little is known about how these elements operate together
within immersive, real -time commerce environments  (Abell & Biswas, 2023; Chen,
Yan & Smith, 2023). Addressing this question allows the study to explain how multiple
antecedents combine to shape dynamic brand experiences and subsequent follower
engagement and impulse buying behaviour, thereby advancing theoretical clarity.

Research Question 2: How do sense of presence and brand –streamer–follower
congruence impact the relationship between streamers’ attributes and dynamic
brand experiences in live-streaming commerce?

The mediating role of psychological mechanisms such as sense of presence and
congruence remains underexplored. While presence has been studied in virtual
environments and congruence in traditional marketing (Zhang, Wang & Zhang, 2021;
Liu et al., 2024) its specific function as mediator in live -streaming contexts is not well

36

established. By asking this question, the study seeks to uncover the processes
through which attractiveness-based streamer cues translate into engagement and
impulse buying behaviour , addressing a critical gap in the consumer psychology of
live streaming.

Research Question 3: To what extent do gender  and product type impact the
relationship between dynamic brand experiences and follower engagement and
impulse buying behaviour outcomes?
This question acknowledges that follower engagement and impulse buying behaviour
responses to live streaming commerce are not uniform. Prior studies suggest that
demographic characteristics (e.g., gender) and product -related factors (e.g., type of
product promoted) may shape the extent to which brand experiences translate into
engagement and purchases (Liu & Yu, 2022; Li, Tang & Feng, 2023) . However, little
is known about how such differences play out in the fast -paced, interactive setting of
live streaming. By examining these moderating influences, this research provides a
deeper understanding of follower engagement and impulse buying behaviour, helping
to explain why some live-streaming strategies succeed with certain groups or product
types while others do not.

Research Question 4: What practical strategies can streamers and brands adopt
to enhance credibility, presence, and congruence, thereby optimising follower
engagement and stimulating impulse purchases during live-streaming sessions?

The final question explicitly addresses the applied side of the research. By linking
theoretical insights to actionable strategies, this study provides value not only to

37

academia but also to practitioners seeking to maximise ROI. This includes exploring
how brands and streamers can design campaigns that leverage credibility cues, foster
immersive presence, and align values with followers to build trust and drive impulse
buying.

1.7 Research Methods
To investigate the psychological and experiential mechanisms that drive follower
engagement and impulse buying behaviour in live streaming commerce, this study
adopts a quantitative research approach. There are 3 reasons.

First, this approach is well -suited to studies aiming to measure relationships among
multiple variables, test hypotheses, and generalise findings across a broader
population (Saunders, Lewis & Thornhill, 2019; Creswell, 2014; Bryman, 2016) . Live
streaming commerce involves complex and interrelated elements such as streamer
attributes, sense of presence, brand –streamer–follower congruence, and dynamic
brand experiences. Given the multifactorial nature of these constructs and their
potential effects on follower engagement and impulse buying  behaviour, the
quantitative research enables the researcher to statistically test these relationships
using structured data collection and analysis techniques (Saunders, Lewis & Thornhill,
2019; Schindler, 2022; D O’Gorman & MacIntosh, 2015) . The quantitative research
method is particularly appropriate for capturing a wide range of perceptions and
behaviours from a large sample, which strengthens the external validity and
generalizability of the results. (Saunders, Lewis & Thornhill, 2019; Schindler, 2022).
Secondly, this study uses the online questionnaire to collect primary data from social
media users in China and the UK, using a 7-point Likert scale to measure the variables.

38

The data is analysed using SPSS and SmartPLS to examine both direct and indirect
relationships between constructs (Bryman, 2016; Schindler, 2022). The questionnaire
is aligned with the study’s objective of developing an empirically grounded framework
that could inform both academic understanding and practical strategies for live
streaming commerce. In addition, this methodological approach aligns closely with the
research objectives and questions by enabling a structured and measurable
assessment of live streaming commerce as a digital marketing strategy. The use of
the questionnaire allows for systematic data collection and provides the foundation for
empirical testing of the proposed relationships between key constructs.

Thirdly, this study targets social media users from both China and the United Kingdom,
two markets that offer distinct yet complementary perspectives on live streaming
commerce. China represents one of the most mature and commercially driven live
streaming markets globally (Shi, Wang & Zhang, 2023) , while the UK reflects an
emerging market where streamer-driven live streaming commerce is gaining
momentum (Merritt & Zhao, 2022; WW, 2025). Including these two contexts enhances
the study's cross -cultural relevance and allows for greater generalisability of the
findings. Moreover, examining these different markets facilitates an understanding of
how cultural and market dynamics may influence follower engagement and impulse
buying behaviour.  This adds an important comparative dimension to the research,
contributing to the broader literature on digital marketing across cultures. (Saunders,
Lewis & Thornhill, 2019; D O’Gorman & MacIntosh, 2015).

39

Therefore, the questionnaire-based approach not only ensures methodological rigour
but also allows for a more comprehensive and culturally explorat ory of the research
questions.

1.8 Contributions
This research offers several important contributions to both academic theory and
marketing practice in the context of live streaming commerce and streamer marketing.

1.8.1 Theoretical Contributions
This research makes several key theoretical contributions to the study of live
streaming commerce and follower engagement and impulse buying behaviour.

First, it advances knowledge by developing an integrated multi -theoretical framework
that combines Source Credibility Theory, Congruity Theory, Flow Theory, and
Consumer Engagement Theory. While these theories have individually been applied
to digital and influencer marketing, their combined application in the highly interactive,
real-time context of live streaming commerce has not previously been examined. By
synthesising these perspectives, the study explains how streamer s’ attributes,
congruence, and  brand experiences collectively drive follower engagement and
impulse buying, thereby moving the literature beyond fragmented single -theory
explanations (Henseler et al., 2016,Hair et al., 2019).

Second, the study makes a contribution by extending Source Credibility Theory and
Congruity Theory into live streaming commerce contexts. Prior applications of these
frameworks primarily emphasised static marketing formats, such as celebrity

40

endorsements or pre -recorded influencer content. By contrast, this study positions
credibility and congruence within live, unscripted interactions between streamers and
audiences, highlighting their dynamic and relational nature. In particular, the finding s
shed light on how congruence among streamers, brands, and followers acts not only
as a static predictor of trust but also as a dynamic mechanism shaping real -time
emotional and cognitive responses (Henseler et al., 2016, Liang et al., 2025).

Third, the research contributes to Flow Theory and Presence literature by
demonstrating how immersive experiences (social presence and telepresence)
mediate the relationship between streamer attributes and consumer outcomes. While
flow and presence have been widely studied in gaming and virtual environments, their
application in live streaming commerce remains limited  (Hsu & Lin, 2021; Li & Peng,
2021). This study shows that the sen se of presence does not simply enhance
enjoyment but serves as a psychological mechanism that amplifies dynamic brand
experiences and facilitates downstream behaviours such as impulse buying. This
extends Flow Theory into the consumer psychology of interactive commerce.

Fourth, the study enhances Consumer Engagement Theory by contextualising
engagement as a multidimensional construct (cognitive, emotional, and behavioural)
within real-time, co-created brand experiences. Unlike static digital environments, live
streaming a llows followers to actively participate in shaping the consumption
experience (Le et al., 2022; Zhang et al., 2025) . This research highlights the reciprocal
and co -creative nature of engagement in live  streaming commerce and positions
dynamic brand experiences as a central driver of these processes, thereby extending
engagement theory into more interactive, psychologically rich environments.

41

Fifth, the research makes a methodological and contextual contribution by empirically
testing mediating mechanisms (sense of presence, brand –streamer–follower
congruence, dynamic brand experiences) and moderating variables (gender, product
type). These dim ensions have been largely overlooked in previous research. By
incorporating them into a unified model, the study responds to calls for more
contextualised and multidimensional frameworks in digital marketing and consumer
psychology (Hair et al., 2019; Henseler et al., 2016).

Finally, the development and empirical validation of the conceptual model across two
culturally distinct contexts (UK and China) contribute to the growing literature on cross-
cultural digital marketing. The findings show not only the universality of certai n
mechanisms but also how contextual differences may shape consumer responses.
This positions the study as a significant step in broadening the external validity and
cultural relevance of psychological theories applied to live streaming commerce.

In sum, this research advances theory by developing a comprehensive and
multidimensional model of follower engagement and impulse buying behaviour in live
streaming commerce, extending established theories into new contexts, identifying
novel mediating and moderating mechanisms, and demonstrating the cross -cultural
robustness of the proposed framework.

1.8.2 Practical Contributions
This study also offers several important practical contributions for streamers, brands,
and marketers seeking to optimise live streaming as a digital marketing and sales
channel.

42

First, by identifying how specific streamer attributes (expertise, trustworthiness,
attractiveness) influence followers’ perceptions and behaviours, the study provides
clear guidance for streamer selection and training. Brands and mark eting agencies
can use these findings to prioritise credibility and authenticity over superficial
popularity when choosing collaborators, ensuring that streamers can effectively build
trust and drive conversion. This also highlights the importance of ongoin g training for
streamers to enhance expertise and authenticity in product presentation.

Second, the study highlights the role of dynamic brand experiences and immersive
presence (social presence and telepresence) as psychological mechanisms driving
engagement and impulse buying. For practitioners, this implies that successful live
streaming campaigns should go beyond product demonstrations and instead focus on
designing interactive, immersive, and emotionally engaging content. This may include
features such as real -time Q&A, personalised responses, and creating a sense of
experience that makes followers feel part of the event.

Third, although gender and product type were examined as potential moderators, the
results show they did not significantly influence the key relationships in this study. This
suggests that such effects may be more strongly shaped by platform or app algorithms
rather than factors that streamers or brands can directly control. For practitioners, this
finding highlights the importance of focusing on controllable elements —such as
enhancing streamer credibility, fostering congruence, and designing immersive
content—rather than overemphasising demographic segmentation or product
category differences, which may be less relevant in algorithm -driven live streaming
environments.

43

Fourth, the study provides actionable guidance on optimising brand –streamer–
follower congruence. The findings suggest that alignment between the streamer’s
persona, the brand’s identity, and the audience’s values strengthens engagement and
reduces sceptici sm. Practically, this encourages marketers to focus on strategic
brand–streamer matching and ensure consistent messaging across campaigns,
thereby fostering long-term brand loyalty rather than short-term sales boosts.
Fifth, the cross-cultural design of the study (UK vs. China) provides practitioners with
insights into international marketing strategies for live streaming commerce. While
certain mechanisms (e.g., presence, dynamic brand experience) appear robust across
cultures, subtle variations highlight the need for context -sensitive adaptations in
campaign design. This supports global brands in localising their live  streaming
commerce strategies to account for cultural differences in consumer expectations,
trust-building, and engagement patterns.

Collectively, these contributions provide businesses with a more evidence -based,
psychologically grounded roadmap for leveraging live streaming commerce. By
focusing on credible streamer attributes, immersive experiences, audience
congruence, and tailored segmentation strategies, firms can enhance follower
engagement, stimulate impulse buying, and maximise returns on their live streaming
investments.

1.9 Thesis Structure
This paper is divided into 6 Chapters. Chapter 1 Introduction: This serves as an
introduction, providing an overview of live streaming commerce study and setting the
stage for the subsequent chapters, providing the outline of the focus of the dissertation.

44

Also, the research problem, potential contributions, limitations, and delimitations and
is identified, focusing on the need to understand the mechanisms that influence
consumer engagement and impulse buying through streamers’ attributes, dynamic
brand experiences, sense of presence and brand follower streamer congruence and
its impact on theories. Also, the specific research questions are proposed, addressing
the gaps in the existing literature and the objectives of the study. The rationale for
adopting a quantitative methods approach is explained, emphasising its suitability for
capturing insights and providing a comprehensive analysis. The chapter concludes by
outlining the structure of the thesis, detailing the subsequent chapters that delve into
the methodology, data analysis, findings, potential contributions, limitations, and
conclusions. By establishing a strong introduction, this chapter sets the foundation for
the research, contextualises the importance of the live streaming commerce topic, and
creates a framework for the subsequent chapters to explore and analyse the research
in depth.

Chapter 2 literature review: This is dedicated to the comprehensive literature review,
which critically examines the existing knowledge related to digital marketing and live
streaming commerce. This chapter begins by providing an overview of the key
concepts and theories relevant to li ve streaming commerce, streamers’ attributes,
dynamic brand experiences, sense of presence and brand -streamer and follower
congruence. It explores the relevant literature, delving into its emergence, evolution,
and impact within the context of live streaming commerce and digital marketing. The
literature review also highlights their significance in understanding the different
aspects that  drive followers' engagement and impulse buying with streamers’
attributes and dynamic brand experiences. The chapter critically analyses relevant

45

studies, identifying gaps, inconsistencies, and debates in the literature, and identifies
the theoretical frameworks that guide the research.

Chapter 3: Hypotheses Development This chapter presents the development of the
study’s hypotheses, building directly on the theoretical foundations and research gaps
identified in Chapter 2. This chapter focuses on articulating the theoretical
relationships between key variables in the conceptual framework, including streamers’
attributes, sense of presence, congruence, dynamic brand experience, follower
engagement, and impulse buying. The hypotheses are organised according to the
Stimulus–Organism–Response (SOR) structure, outlining how streamers’ attributes
operate as stimuli, how psychological mechanisms such as sense of presence and
congruence function as organismic responses, and how dynamic brand experiences
lead to engagement and impulse buying . Additionally, this chapter introduces the
moderating roles of gender and product type, explaining their potential influence on
the dynamic brand experience –behaviour relationship. Collectively, the hypotheses
presented in this chapter establish the analytical foundation for the empirical testing in
the subsequent methodology and findings chapters.

Chapter 4 methodology: This focuses on the research methodology. This chapter is
based on the Research Onion and outlines the research design, data collection
procedures, and data analysis techniques utilised in the study. The chapter discusses
the quantitative research methods approach adopted, emphasising how it enables the
integration of quantitative data to provide a comprehensive understanding of the
research topic. The specific data collection methods, such as online  questionnaires,
are described, along with the rationale for selecting these methods and the target

46

population in the UK and China. The chapter concludes by outlining the structure of
the thesis, detailing the subsequent chapters that delve into the methodology, data
analysis, findings, potential contributions, limitations, delimitations, and conclusions.
By establishing a strong introduction, this chapter sets the foundation for the research,
contextualises the importance of the topic, and creates a framework for the
subsequent chapters to explore and analyse the live streaming commerce research
in-depth.

Chapter 5 data analysis: this focuses on the data analysis the steps taken to transcribe
and organise quantitative data, the chapter explains the statistical analyses conducted,
such as descriptive statistics, regression analysis, or correlation analysis, to examine
the relationships between streamers’ attributes, dynamic brand experiences, sense of
presence, streamer follower brand experience, follower engagement and impulse
buying and trends within the data. Then, all hypotheses were tested to see if they were
supported, in order to achieve a comprehensive analysis and deep understanding of
this study.

Chapter 6 Findings and discussion: This focuses on the live  streaming commerce
research findings and discussion, providing  an in -depth discussion based on the
research. The chapter discusses the main findings, provides a comprehensive
overview of the results obtained from the quantitative data, and the findings addresses
each research question and objective. After presenting the findings, the chapter dives
into a detailed discussion and interpretation of the results. The discussion section
explores the implications of live streaming commerce and follower engagement and
impulse buying  behaviour of the findings, connects them to existing literature and

47

theoretical frameworks, and provides insights into the live streaming commerce
research. In addition, this chapter answers the  research objectives and key findings.
Then discusses the theoretical and practical contributions of the study, emphasising
how it fills gaps in the existing live streaming commerce literature and extends our
understanding of the research topic. Furthermore, this chapter acknowledges the
limitations of the study as well as points to future research directions and opportunities
in the live streaming commerce area.

1.10 Summary
Chapter 1 has established the importance of investigating live streaming commerce
as an emerging digital marketing phenomenon that uniquely combines real -time
interaction, product promotion, and consumer engagement. The chapter highlighted
the research pro blem, namely the limited understanding of the psychological and
behavioural mechanisms through which streamer attributes, sense of presence, and
brand–streamer–follower congruence influence dynamic brand experiences, follower
engagement, and impulse buying.

It also positioned the study within consumer psychology, emphasising the need to
move beyond descriptive accounts of live streaming commerce toward a theoretically
grounded model that integrates different theories. In doing so, the chapter identified
clear research objectives and questions that focus on unpacking both the direct and
indirect pathways shaping consumer responses in live commerce environments.

Furthermore, the potential contributions were outlined, showing how this research
extends theoretical debates on credibility, congruence, and experiential marketing

48

while also offering practical insights for brands, marketers, and streamers seeking to
design more effective live commerce strategies. Finally, the scope and delimitations
clarified the study’s boundaries, particularly its psychological focus and cross-cultural
perspective, ensuring a coherent and rigorous approach to examining this rapidly
evolving marketing practice.

The next chapter will build on this foundation by presenting a literature review to
critically analyse existing research and position the study within the broader academic
context.

49

2 Chapter 2: Literature Review
The purpose of this literature review is to critically examine existing research on digital
marketing and live streaming commerce, focusing on how streamers’ attributes
influence follower engagement and impulse buying through key psychological and
experiential mechanisms. This review aims to establish a strong theoretical and
conceptual foundation for the study by identifying core constructs, highlighting
research gaps, and demonstrating how the current research contributes to advancing
knowledge in this emerging field.

To ensure a rigorous and comprehensive review, key academic databases such as
Scopus and Google Scholar were used to source peer -reviewed journal articles, with
emphasis on recent studies in live streaming commerce, digital marketing, and
engagement and impulse buying behaviour. The inclusion criteria focused on research
related to streamer credibility, sense of presence, congruence, and dynamic brand
experiences, supported by relevant theoretical frameworks including Source
Credibility Theory, Congruity Theory, and Flow Theory.

The review argues that live streaming commerce represents a transformative evolution
in digital marketing, where real-time interaction, streamer credibility, and psychological
engagement converge to shape consumer decision-making and behaviour. It explores
how streamers’ attributes —such as attractiveness, trustworthiness, and expertise —
alongside mediating factors like social and telepresence, as well as brand–streamer–
follower congruence, influence consumer trust, engagement, and impulse purchases.

50

By drawing on this research from marketing, psychology, and consumer behaviour
studies, this review integrates multiple theoretical perspectives to offer a
comprehensive framework for understanding the interactive and immersive nature of
live streaming commerce. Synthesising these insights allows the study to identify
critical gaps i n existing literature, such as the underexplored mediating roles of
presence and congruence, thereby laying the groundwork for the conceptual model
and hypotheses presented in the following sections.

2.1 Context Background: Live Streaming Commerce
2.1.1 Definition of Live Streaming Commerce
Live streaming commerce is a digital marketing strategy that integrates real-time video
broadcasting with interactive, transactional and immersive consumer experiences (Ma,
Zou & Lv, 2022; Gu, Cheng & Shen, 2023; Li & Lee, 2024; Chung et al., 2025) . It
enables brands and streamers to promote and demonstrate products live while
engaging directly with audiences through two -way communication tools (Liu & Yu,
2022; Deng, Chen & Wu, 2024; Deng & and Wu, 2024; Li & Lee, 2024) . This format
has evolved from traditional static e -commerce by embedding entertainment,
immediacy, and social interaction into the shopping journey, creating a more
immersive and persuasive environment for consumers (Li et al., 2023; Picot -Coupey
et al., 2023; Xin, Liu & Jian, 2024).

2.1.2 Growth and Relevance of Live Streaming Commerce.
The growth of live streaming commerce has been driven by advancements in mobile
technology, social media platforms, and user-generated content (Hajarian et al., 2021;
Chen, Pham & Tran, 2024) . Recent systematic reviews  in live streaming commerce

51

has rapidly emerged as a powerful digital marketing channel that merges real -time
video broadcasting with interactive viewing and shopping experiences  (Luo et al.,
2023; Fauzi et al., 2026). First popularised in China through platforms like Taobao Live
and later adopted globally by platforms such as TikTok, Instagram, and YouTube, live
streaming commerce allows streamers and brands to showcase products, interact with
viewers, and drive purchasing decisions in real time (Yan et al., 2022; Deng, Chen &
Wu, 2024; Lee & and Lee, 2025) . Unlike traditional e -commerce, live streaming
commerce is distinguished by its immediacy, interactivity, and entertainment value,
which together foster deeper consumer involvement and increase the likelihood of
engagement and impulse buying (Yu, Cheah & Liu, 2022; Hua et al., 2023; Huang,
Kim & Ko, 2024).

Also, some scholars have identified live streaming commerce as online entertainment,
social interaction, and commercial transactions (Xi et al., 2023). Streamers often play
a dual role as both content creators and persuasive sellers, using their charisma,
expertise, and real-time responsiveness to influence follower attitudes and behaviours
(Luo et al., 2023; Lu, Wu & Zhao, 2022; Xue & Liu, 2022). The interactivity inherent in
live streaming—enabled through features like live chat, instant feedback, and Q&A —
encourages social presence, which is believed to strengthen trust and engagement
(Khan et al., 2025). Additionally, limited time offers and flash sales often intensify the
urgency to purchase, further differentiating live streaming commerce from traditional
digital marketing formats (Lai, Aw & Tan, 2024; Lin, Gui & Lu, 2024; Li et al., 2025).

Despite its growing popularity and commercial impact, live streaming commerce
remains an evolving research area, especially concerning the psychological

52

mechanisms that drive follower engagement and purchasing decisions. Overall, live
streaming commerce has evolved into a global marketing and retail phenomenon with
substantial economic influence. In China, live  streaming commerce accounts for a
rapidly increasing share of e -commerce sales, driven by the popularity of platforms
like Taobao Live and Douyin (Deng, Chen & Wu, 2024). Western markets are also
catching up, with TikTok Shop, YouTube Live, and Instagram integratin g shopping
features that allow consumers to purchase directly during broadcasts (Lee & Lee,
2025). Recent industry estimates suggest that global live streaming commerce
revenues could exceed USD 1 trillion by 2026, underscoring its position as one of the
fastest-growing sectors in digital marketing. The scale and momentum of this growth
demonstrate that live streaming commerce is not a passing trend but a significant,
enduring shift in consumer engagement and purchasing behaviour worldwide.

Given the global expansion of live streaming commerce, understanding cross-cultural
differences has become increasingly important. According to Hofstede’s cultural
dimensions (Hofstede, 2001; Hofstede et al., 2010), China and the United Kingdom
differ in cultural values such as individualism, collectivism, and long -term orientation,
which may influence how followers perceive streamers, engage with live streaming
content, and make purchasing decisions. Therefore, comparing these two countries
provides a meaningful context for examining whether the proposed relationships are
consistent across different cultural settings.

2.1.3 Differences From Traditional Influencer Marketing.
When discussing live streaming commerce, it is easy to get  confused with influencer
marketing, but there is a difference . While traditional influencer marketing typically

53

relies on pre -produced content such as sponsored posts, brand endorsements, and
curated videos or images (Lou & Yuan, 2019; Kapitan et al., 2022; Leung, Gu &
Palmatier, 2022) , live streaming commerce introduces a fundamentally different
dynamic through its real -time, interactive format. In traditional models, influencer
content is often polished, delayed, and limited in interactivity, providing consumers
with a passive viewing experience. By contrast, live streaming creates a synchronous
communication environment, enabling viewers to engage in real -time conversations,
ask questions, and observe unscripted product demonstrations (Kang et al., 2021; He
et al., 2023). This immediacy fosters a heightened sense of presence and perceived
authenticity, which c ould enhance consumer trust and influence impulsive buying
(Ming et al., 2021; Guo et al., 2021; Shao, 2023).

Additionally, live streaming allows for greater emotional connection and
responsiveness, as streamers c ould adapt their messaging based on live feedback,
incorporate viewer comments into their narrative, and create a participatory brand
experience(Shao, 2023; Lai, Aw & Tan, 2024; Wang, 2024) . This level of interactivity
is rarely achievable in static influencer formats, where engagement tends to be limited
to delayed likes, comments, or reposts. Moreover, the entertainment value and
temporal urgency created through flash sales, limited-time offers, and exclusive live
deals are unique to live streaming commerce and often serve as psychological triggers
for immediate decision  making (Lai, Aw & Tan, 2024; Lin, Gui & Lu, 2024; Li et al.,
2025).

Also, from a theoretical standpoint, these differences also suggest that traditional
influencer models—grounded primarily in Source Credibility Theory and persuasion

54

frameworks—may not fully capture the interactive, experiential, and immersive nature
of live streaming. This is because Source Credibility Theory primarily focuses on static
cues such as trustworthiness, attractiveness, and expertise (Hovland & Weiss, 1951 ;
Ohanian, 1990), which operate in one -way communication environments where
consumers passively evaluate influencer content. However, live streaming commerce
involves dynamic, two-way communication in which consumers actively co-create the
experience throu gh real -time interaction, emotional feedback, and collective
participation (Guo et al., 2021; Kang et al., 2021). In this context, consumer
perceptions and purchase intentions are shaped not only by the influencer’s credibility
but also by psychological me chanisms such as sense of presence, flow, and
congruence among the streamer, follower, and brand. These interactive and
situational dynamics extend beyond the explanatory scope of traditional persuasion
models, highlighting the need for an integrated theoretical framework that reflects the
immediacy and participatory nature of live streaming commerce.

Therefore, a more integrated approach that combines sense of presence, congruence,
and dynamic engagement mechanisms is needed to explain follower behaviour in this
evolving space. These theoretical perspectives are particularly applicable because
they collectively capture the multi -dimensional nature of live streaming interactions.
The sense of presence framework explains how real -time interactivity and immersion
heighten consumers’ emotional and cognitive involvement (Ming et al., 2021; Shi, Li &
Hu, 2023), while congruence theory clarifies how alignment between the streamer’s
persona, the brand’s image, and the follower’s identity enhances authenticity and trust
(Sirgy, 1982; Aaker, 1999). Meanwhile, dynamic engagement mechanisms, grounded
in experiential marketing and SOR theory, account for how stimuli within the live

55

streaming environment—such as real -time communication, visual cues, and shared
experiences—trigger internal psychological responses that lead to engagement and
impulse buying (Brodie et al., 2011; Mehrabian & Russell, 1974). Together, these
frameworks provide a more comprehensive and contextually relevant explanation of
follower engagement and impulse buying  behaviour in live streaming commerce,
where interaction, alignment, and immersion operate simultaneously to shape
decision-making.
So, this study seeks to fill that gap by critically examining live streaming commerce as
a distinct phenomenon, rather than a simple extension of traditional influencer
practices.

2.1.4 Shaping Live Streaming Commerce: The Roles of Brands, Streamers, and
Followers
To illustrate how the key actors in live streaming commerce interact, Figure 2 presents
a conceptual interpretation of the relationships among brands, streamers, and
followers. While informed by existing literature, the diagram reflects a synthesised
understanding of the interconnections among these three parties within the live
streaming environment. It highlights the bidirectional interaction between followers and
streamers, the streamer’s role in shaping and conveying brand meaning, and the ways
in which  brand cues inform follower perceptions. By mapping these interconnected
pathways, the figure clarifies the relational foundations that underpin the development
of the subsequent conceptual model and hypotheses.

56

Figure 2:Conceptual representation of the interconnections among brands, streamers,
and followers in live streaming commerce

2.1.4.1 The Role of The Brand in Shaping the Interactions Between Streamers and
Followers.
In the domain of live streaming commerce, the interplay between brands, streamers,
and followers is dynamic and multifaceted. Brands are not mere bystanders in the live
streaming marketing arena; rather, they actively shape the interactions between
streamers and their followers. For example, beauty brands such as L’Oréal or Perfect
Diary often collaborate with professional streamers to design live streaming sessions
that highlight specific product narratives, coordinate promotional timing, and even
script key talking points to ensure message consistency. Through these strategic
interventions, brands influence not only how products  are presented but also how
engagement unfolds, reinforcing brand identity and shaping consumer perceptions in
real time.

On the one hand, the selection of streamers by brands is a strategic process
underpinned by a set of criteria that goes beyond mere popularity metrics (Wang et

57

al., 2021; Leung et al., 2022; Xue & Liu, 2022). Brands look for streamers whose brand
image and follower demographics align with their marketing objectives (Chen, 2021;
Li, Tang & Feng, 2023) . For example, Nike often partners with fitness and lifestyle
streamers who embody an active and aspirational image, ensuring that the streamer’s
persona and audience interests resonate with the brand’s core message of
performance and motivation.

This alignment is important to ensuring that the brand essence is congruent with the
streamer's content, thereby avoiding dissonance within the target followers (Ki et al.,
2020; Chopra, Avhad & Jaju, 2021) . In selecting streamers, brands often employ
analytics of follower engagement rate and other follower behaviours (Wies, Bleier &
Edeling, 2022; Tafesse & Wood, 2021; Delbaere, Michael & Phillips, 2021) , but they
also consider qualitative factors such as content authenticity and the streamer's
reputation (Kapitan et al., 2022; Chen, Yan & Smith, 2023) . Once selected, the
collaboration between brands and streamers takes various forms, ranging from
sponsored content to collaborative product launches (Hughes, Swaminathan &
Brooks, 2019; Delbaere, Michael & Phillips, 2021) . Brands and streamers co -create
content that seamlessly integrates product placements and brand messages into the
streamer's narrative, fostering a more organic and engaging user experience (Giertz
et al., 2021; Xue & Liu, 2022) . The success of these collaborations often hinges on
the streamer's creativity, with brands providing guidance while still allowing streamers
to maintain their unique style and voice (Hughes, Swaminathan & Brooks, 2019;
Borchers & Enke, 2021; Roggeveen et al., 2021;Shao et al., 2026).

58

On the other hand, engagement with followers is another important aspect of brand
strategy in live streaming commerce. Brands are increasingly leveraging interactive
tools provided by live streaming platforms to engage directly with the audience. This
engagement takes the form of real -time chats, polls, and question and answer
sessions, allowing brands to gather immediat e feedback and foster a sense of
community (Kang et al., 2021; He et al., 2023). Moreover, brands often initiate hashtag
challenges, giveaways, and other interactive campaigns to incentivise follower
participation and content sharing, thus amplifying their reach and presence in the
digital space (Giertz et al., 2021; Jiang, Guangkuo & Xuezheng, 2022; Jones & Lee,
2022; Li, Tang & Feng, 2023) . Such strategies not only drive engagement but also
create valuable user-generated content that enhances the brand's visibility and appeal
(Casidy, Helmi & Bridson, 2019; Hughes, Swaminathan & Brooks, 2019; Moran,
Muzellec & Johnson, 2019).

The influence of the brand extends to the relationship between streamers and
followers, where the brand acts as a mediator and facilitator of community bonds
(Kucharska, 2019; Ki et al., 2020) . Brands set the stage for how streamers and
followers interact, often defining the context in which these interactions occur. For
example, the brand sponsored event provide d a platform for streamers to showcase
their expertise while simultaneously engaging followers in a collective experience that
strengthens their relationship (Hughes, Swaminathan & Brooks, 2019; Chen, Yan &
Smith, 2023) . The brand's impact is also seen in how it supports streamers in
cultivating a loyal following. By providing resources, content, and opportunities for
streamers to elevate their presence, brands indirectly enhance the streamer follower
connection. This sy mbiotic relationship is further enriched when followers become

59

brand advocates, often spurred by the streamer's endorsement and the brand's active
community engagement efforts (Jun & Yi, 2020; Ki et al., 2020; Chen, 2021) . The
brand's role in affecting the streamer -follower relationship is multifaceted, influencing
not just the direct interactions but also the underlying sentiment within the online
community (Delbaere, Michael & Phillips, 2021; Cheung et al., 2022; Wei et al., 2022).
So, the brand's role in affecting the streamer-follower relationship is multifaceted.

2.1.4.2 The Role of the Streamer in Shaping the Interactions Between Brands and
Followers
In the interplay of live streaming commerce, streamers command a central role,
shaping the interactions between brands and followers with finesse and strategic
intent. Their collaboration with brands allows streamers not only to showcase products
but also to integrate brand narratives into their personal streaming (Eng & Jarvis, 2020;
Chopra, Avhad & Jaju, 2021; Li, Tang & Feng, 2023). This symbiosis allows streamers
to maintain authenticity while serving as brand ambassadors (Xue & Liu, 2022; Li,
Tang & Feng, 2023) . The manner in which streamers collaborate with brands often
reflects their personal brand alignment and the needs of their followers, creating a
tailored fit that feels genuine to the audience (Leung, Gu & Palmatier, 2022; Wong &
Wei, 2023) . This authenticity is key, as it fosters trust and credibility, essential
components in influencing followers' perceptions and behaviours (He, Li & Xue, 2022;
Yan et al., 2022; Li et al., 2023; Zhou & Lou, 2023).

Streamers navigate an important position in live streaming commerce, acting as both
content creators and influential personalities. Their collaboration with brands is not a
one-way endorsement but a multifaceted partnership (Caruelle, 2023) . They bring

60

brand narratives to life, infusing them with personal anecdotes and genuine
recommendations that resonate with their followers (Wong & Wei, 2023; Lee & Wan,
2023). This approach to content creation fosters a sense of trust and relatability, which
is crucial for brand messaging to be well -received (Leite & Baptista, 2022; Holiday et
al., 2023).

When streamers engage with followers, streamers leverage their approach to create
interactive and compelling content (Dou et al., 2023; Gu, Zhang & Kannan, 2023; Li et
