& Shang, 2021; Dhaigude & Mohan, 2022; Liu et al., 2023; Khoi, Le & Nguyen Dong,
2023; Khoi & Le, 2023) . Only a few studies have systematically explored how these
effects occur through interactive and experiential mechanisms such as sense of
presence and congruence. This gap in understanding led to the development of a
conceptual model that integrates Source Credibility Theory, Congruence Theory, Flow
Theory, Engagement theory and the Stimulus –Organism–Response (S -O-R)
framework to explain engagement and impulse buying behaviour in live streaming
contexts.

107

Specifically, there are our major research gaps were addressed through the
hypotheses formulated in this chapter:  Gap 1: The insufficient exploration of how
different theories integrate in the live streaming commerce environment to understand
engagement, impulse buying  behaviour, psychological and contextual mechanisms.
Gap 2: The limited understanding of how streamer attributes influence the sense of
presence and congruence. Gap 3: The lack of research on the sense of presence as
a mediator to test how telepresence enhances dynamic brand experience and follower
engagement and impulse buying  behaviour. Gap 4: The lack of research on
congruence to test how alignment between these elements enhances dynamic brand
experience and follower engagement and impulse buying  behaviour. Gap 5: The
insufficient exploration of dynamic brand experience as a psychological mechanism
assessing its effects on follower engagement and impulse buying.

By synthesising these findings, this chapter advanced theoretical understanding by
positioning live streaming commerce as a dynamic and interactive marketing
environment, where streamer credibility, sense of presence, and congruence
collectively drive engagement and purchasing behaviours. Overall, the chapter
provided a coherent theoretical rationale for the research gaps and model, establishing
a solid foundation for the hypotheses development presented in the next chapter.

108

Chapter 3: Hypotheses Development
Building upon the theoretical foundations and research gaps identified in the previous
chapter, this chapter develops the hypotheses that underpin the conceptual framework
of the study. While Chapter 2 critically reviewed existing literature to establish the key
constructs—streamers’ attributes, sense of presence, brand –streamer–follower
congruence, dynamic brand experience, follower engagement, and impulse buying —
this chapter focuses on articulating the theoretical relationships between these
constructs. The objective is to move beyond description and to explain how and why
these constructs interact within the context of live streaming commerce.

Drawing on the Stimulus –Organism–Response (SOR) framework, the Source
Credibility Theory, and the Congruence Theory, Flow Theory and Customer
Engagement theory, this chapter conceptualises streamers’ attributes as external
stimuli that shape consumers’ int ernal psychological states, represented by sense of
presence, congruence, and dynamic brand experiences. These internal states, in turn,
influence follower engagement and impulse buying behaviour. The integration of these
theories enables a multidimensiona l understanding of follower engagement and
impulse buying behaviour in live streaming environments, where interaction,
authenticity, and experiential immersion converge to drive engagement and purchase
decisions.

In doing so, this chapter establishes the logical and theoretical foundations for each
hypothesised relationship in the model. The following sections systematically present
and justify the proposed hypotheses, linking each relationship to relevant theoreti cal

109

perspectives and empirical findings to demonstrate how this research advances
existing knowledge in digital marketing and live streaming commerce.

2.10 Streamers’ Attributes and Dynamic Brand Experience
2.10.1 Streamers’ attributes and Dynamic Brand Experiences
In live streaming commerce, streamers’ attributes play a central role in shaping
followers’ perceptions and experiences with brands. Streamers act as human
interfaces for brands, and their credibility, authenticity and expertise directly influence
how followers interpret and internalise brand -related cues. According to the Source
Credibility Model (Hovland et al., 1953; Ohanian, 1990), credible communicators
enhance message acceptance by strengthening consumers’ cognitive and affective
responses. Similarly,  authentic and approachable streamers foster emotional
closeness and trust, which enhances followers’ receptivity to brand information
delivered in real time. These mechanisms feed into the broader experience of the
brand during live streams.

Within the SOR framework, streamers’ attributes function as stimuli that elicit followers’
internal processing —shaping sensory, affective, behavioural, and intellectual brand
experiences (Mehrabian & Russell, 1974; Wang et al., 2022). When followers perceive
a streamer as trustworthy, knowledgeable, and authentic, they process brand
information more deeply, experience stronger emotional resonance, and perceive the
brand encounter as more engaging and immersive. This alignment between streamer
qualities and followers’ experiential responses reinforces the dynamic brand
experience, which is multimodal and evolves across the live session through visual

110

demonstrations, narrative storytelling, and interactive exchanges.  Given this
theoretical foundation, the following hypothesis is proposed.

H1: Streamers’ attributes positively influence followers’ dynamic brand experience.

2.10.2 Streamers’ Attributes and Sense of Presence
In live streaming commerce, streamers’ attributes play an important role in shaping
followers’ psychological experiences, particularly their sense of presence. Sense of
presence refers to the extent to which followers feel psychologically immersed and
connected to the streamer and the live environment, as if they were physically co -
located in the same space (Steuer, 1992; Sun et al., 2019). According to the Stimulus–
Organism–Response (SOR) framework, external stimuli such as streamers’
appearance, expertise, and credibility act as environmental cues that impact emotional
and cognitive responses from consumers, fostering perceptions of immediacy and co-
presence (Mehrabian & Russell, 1974; Xue et al., 2020).

Streamers who demonstrate credibility, expertise , and interactive responsiveness
enhance followers’ sense of being “present” within the live streaming (Liu et al., 2023;
Ming et al., 2021). For instance, a streamer who displays expertise through clear
product demonstrations or authentic enthusiasm can e voke a stronger emotional
connection, leading followers to perceive the interaction as vivid and real. Similarly,
streamers’ attractiveness and expressiveness enhance visual and emotional
engagement, stimulating telepresence—the psychological state of being absorbed in
the mediated environment (He, Li & Xue, 2022; Tian & Frank, 2024).

111

In this context, streamers’ attributes function as powerful perceptual stimuli that trigger
psychological immersion and co -experiential engagement. Thus, it is hypothesised
that:

H2: Streamers’ attributes have a positive effect on the sense of presence.

2.10.3 Sense of Presence and Dynamic Brand Experience

Sense of presence serves as a fundamental psychological mechanism that enhances
followers’ dynamic brand experiences in live streaming commerce. Defined as the
subjective feeling of “being there” in a mediated environment, sense of presence
consists of soc ial presence —the perception of mutual awareness and real -time
interpersonal connection —and telepresence —the immersive sensation of
experiencing a mediated environment as real (Ming et al., 2021; Ma, Zou & Lv, 2022;
Shi, Li & Hu, 2023; Tian & Frank, 2024). These dimensions collectively enable
followers to engage both emotionally and cognitively with the live streaming,
transforming an impressive viewing experience into an interactive and experiential
encounter.

In the context of live streaming commerce, a heightened sense of presence allows
followers to feel psychologically closer to both the streamer and the brand. When
streamers actively interact with followers —through personalised responses, eye
contact, and emotional expressiveness—the viewers experience stronger feelings of
co-presence and immersion. This, in turn, enhances the dynamic brand experience,
as followers process the brand through multisensory, affective, and intellectual

112

engagement rather than purely functional evaluation (Liao et al., 2022; Yu et al., 2021;
Wang et al., 2022).

From a theoretical perspective, this relationship aligns with Flow Theory, which
proposes that immersion and attentional absorption in engaging environments foster
enhanced experiential states (Csikszentmihalyi, 1990; Huo et al., 2023). Within the
SOR fram ework, sense of presence functions as an organism -level psychological
state. It represents followers’ internal feelings of immersion, co -presence, and
perceptual realism during live streams. These internal experiential reactions deepen
emotional involvemen t and cognitive participation, thereby strengthening followers’
dynamic brand experience. When followers feel socially connected to the streamer or
perceptually immersed in the live environment, they are more likely to interpret brand-
related information positively and experience richer sensory, affective, and intellectual
brand responses. Accordingly, it is hypothesised that:

H3: Sense of presence positively influences followers’ dynamic brand experience.

2.10.4 Mediating Role of Sense of Presence
The relationship between streamers’ attributes and followers’ dynamic brand
experiences operates significantly through the mediating role of sense of presence.
Within the Stimulus –Organism–Response (SOR) framework, streamers’ attributes
function as externa l stimuli (S) that trigger internal psychological mechanisms (O),
such as a heightened sense of presence, which subsequently shape deeper
experiential states like dynamic brand experience (Mehrabian & Russell, 1974; Sun et
al., 2019).

113

A strong sense of presence —comprising both social presence (the feeling of mutual
awareness and interpersonal interaction) and telepresence (the sensation of
immersion in a mediated environment)—enables followers to feel connected, engaged,
and emotionally involved during live streaming sessions (Ming et al., 2021; Ma, Zou &
Lv, 2022; Tian & Frank, 2024). Streamers who demonstrate credibility, expertise, and
interactive communication cultivate an environment where followers perceive the live
experience as vivid, personal, and authentic. This immersive connection enhances
followers’ affective and sensory pro cessing of the brand, reinforcing their overall
dynamic brand experience.

From a theoretical standpoint, this mediation aligns with Flow Theory, which posits
that immersive and interactive environments foster experiential absorption and
emotional resonance (Csikszentmihalyi, 1990; Shi, Li & Hu, 2023). Streamers’
attributes there fore act as booster that impacts presence driven immersion an
organismic process that strengthens followers’ dynamic brand experiences through
heightened emotional and sensory engagement. Accordingly, the following hypothesis
is proposed:

H4: Sense of presence mediates the relationship between streamers’ attributes and
followers’ dynamic brand experience.

2.11 The Role of Brand –Streamer–Follower Congruence on the
relationship between Streamers’ Attributes and DBE

114

2.11.1 Streamers’ Attributes and Congruence
In live streaming commerce, streamers’ attributes play a central role in shaping the
perceived alignment among the streamer, the brand, and their followers. In detail,
congruence refers to the degree of compatibility or consistency between the
personalities, values, and images of these three entities (Osgood & Tannenbaum,
1955; Sirgy, 1982). Drawing on Congruence Theory, individuals are more likely to
develop favourable attitudes and behaviours when they perceive harmony between
their own identity and the characteristics of the source or brand they engage with
(Aaker, 1999; Rollin, 2022).

Streamers’ attributes —such as credibility, expertise, and attractiveness —act as
critical cues that influence this perception of fit. When streamers embody qualities that
resonate with a brand’s image and the values of their audience, followers are more
likely to perceive congruence in the triadic relationship of brand –streamer–follower
(Deng, Chen & Wu, 2024; Zhang & Hu, 2024). For example, an authentic and
knowledgeable streamer who promotes eco -friendly beauty products may reinforce
the perceived alignment between sustainable brand values and followers who share
similar lifestyle preferences. In contrast, a mismatch between streamer persona and
brand identity can create dissonance, weakening consumer identification and
engagement (Venciute et al., 2023; Opić & Pandža Bajs, 2024).

From the Stimulus –Organism–Response (SOR) perspective, streamers’ attributes
serve as external stimuli that trigger cognitive and emotional evaluations of
consistency, leading to stronger perceptions of identity fit among the brand, streamer,
and audience (Liang & Yoon, 2022; Ceylan & Hayran, 2025). This alignment not only

115

strengthens relational bonds but also enhances the authenticity and persuasiveness
of marketing messages in live streaming environments. Accordingly, it is hypothesised
that:

H5: Streamers’ attributes positively influence brand–streamer–follower congruence.

2.11.2 Congruence and Dynamic Brand Experience
Congruence plays an important role in shaping followers’ dynamic brand experiences
by fostering psychological alignment among the streamer, the brand, and the
followers. Rooted in Congruence Theory (Osgood & Tannenbaum, 1955; Sirgy, 1982),
this construct refers to the perceived consistency between the identities, values, and
personalities of these three entities. When followers perceive a strong alignment —
believing that the streamer authentically embodies the brand’s image and that both
reflect their own self -concept—they experience emotional harmony and cognitive
fluency, which enhance the depth and positivity of their brand experience (Abosag et
al., 2020; Venciute et al., 2023; Deng, Chen & Wu, 2024).

In live streaming commerce, congruence operates as a powerful psychological
mechanism that reinforces authenticity and coherence in the brand narrative. When
the streamer’s communication style, tone, and persona align with the brand’s
positioning and the a udience’s expectations, followers are more likely to internalise
brand values and form stronger emotional bonds. This alignment transforms brand
exposure into a dynamic brand experience —one that is affectively engaging,

116

cognitively stimulating, and experientially immersive (Liang & Yoon, 2022; Ji, Liu &
Chen, 2023; Zhang & Hu, 2024).

From a theoretical standpoint, this relationship aligns with the Stimulus –Organism–
Response (SOR) model, in which streamer –brand–follower congruence operates as
an organism-level mechanism. Congruence reflects followers’ internal cognitive and
affective evaluations of “fit” between themselves, the streamer, and the brand. When
followers perceive a high degree of alignment, this internal appraisal enhances
emotional resonance, reduces psychological distance, and facilitates a more coherent
processing of brand meaning. As an organism construct, congruence shapes the way
followers interpret and internalise the stimuli presented by the streamer, thereby
strengthening their overall dynamic brand experience, which is also an organism-level
outcome. Accordingly, it is hypothesised that:

H6: Brand –streamer–follower congruence positively influences  followers’ dynamic
brand experience.

2.11.3 Mediating Role of Brand–streamer–follower Congruence
In live streaming commerce, congruence operates as a critical psychological
mechanism that mediates the relationship between streamers’ attributes and followers’
dynamic brand experiences. Drawing on Congruence Theory (Osgood & Tannenbaum,
1955; Sirgy, 1982), congruence refers to the perceived alignment among the identities,
values, and personalities of the streamer, the brand, and the followers. When followers

117

perceive this alignment, they experience psychological harmony and authenticity,
which enhances their emotional attachment and deepens their experiential connection
with the brand (Venciute et al., 2023; Deng, Chen & Wu, 2024).
Streamers’ attributes—such as expertise, credibility, and attractiveness —serve as a
foundation that fosters this congruence. When streamers authentically represent the
brand’s values and communicate in ways that resonate with followers’ self concepts
and lifestyles, the perceived fit among these three entities is strengthened. This sense
of fit creates a more coherent and meaningful experience, prompting followers to
strengthen the brand’s identity and form stronger emotional bonds with both the
streamer and the brand (Ji, Liu & Chen, 2023; Liang & Yoon, 2022; Zhang & Hu, 2024).
From the Stimulus–Organism–Response (SOR) perspective, streamers’ attributes act
as external stimuli (S) that evoke cognitive and affective evaluations of fit (O), which
in turn enhance followers’ dynamic brand experiences. This mediating process
explains why congruence facilitates more immersive and emotionally engaging
experiences, as followers feel that their values and identities are reflected in both the
streamer and the brand. Accordingly, the following hypothesis is proposed:

H7: Brand –streamer–follower congruence mediates the relationship between
streamers’ attributes and followers’ dynamic brand experience.

2.12 Dynamic Brand Experience and Behavioural Outcomes

118

2.12.1 Dynamic Brand Experiences and followers’ engagement
Dynamic brand experience represents a multidimensional construct that captures
followers’ sensory, affective, intellectual, and behavioural engagement with a brand
during live streaming. It reflects how consumers internalise brand -related stimuli
presented by streamers and translate them into meaningful experiential responses
(Brakus, Schmitt & Zarantonello , 2009; Wang et al., 2022; Khoi, Le & Nguyen Dong,
2023). Within the Stimulus –Organism–Response (SOR) framework, dynamic brand
experience serves as the organismic state through which external stimuli —such as
streamers’ attributes are psychologically proces sed before influencing behavioural
responses like engagement and impulse buying.

When followers experience a strong dynamic brand experience, they are more likely
to feel emotionally connected to the brand and cognitively invested in the live
streaming process. This immersive state encourages followers to interact more
actively—by commenting, liking, or sharing and to develop long-term identification with
the brand community (Brodie et al., 2011; Yu et al., 2021; Valmohammadi et al., 2024).
A compelling brand experience transforms the viewing process from passive
observation to active participation, fostering engagement as a reciprocal exchange
between the follower, the streamer, and the brand.  Accordingly, the following
hypothesis is proposed:

H8: Dynamic brand experience positively influences followers’ engagement.

119

2.12.2 Dynamic Brand Experiences and Impulse Buying
In live streaming commerce, dynamic brand experience serves as a key psychological
driver of impulse buying behaviour, transforming spontaneous emotions and
immersive experiences into unplanned purchase actions. When followers perceive a
vivid, emotionally  engaging, and cognitively stimulating brand experience, they are
more likely to experience heightened excitement and reduced cognitive control —
conditions that make impulsive purchases more probable (Xu, Huang & Shang, 2021;
Dhaigude & Mohan, 2022; Khoi, Le & Nguyen Dong, 2023).

Dynamic brand experience operates across multiple dimensions— behavioural
sensory, affective, and intellectual, creating a state of psychological involvement that
bridges entertainment and consumption. Sensory stimulation through high -quality
visuals and sound, emotional resonance elicited by relatable streamer narratives, and
cognitive engagement through real -time product demonstrations together foster a
sense of immediacy and enjoyment. This immersive experience diminishes rational
evaluation and triggers an effective response that drives immediate buying behaviour
(Liu et al., 2023; Sarker & Dwivedi, 2024).

From a Stimulus–Organism–Response (SOR) perspective, streamers’ attributes and
live interactions act as external stimuli (S) that activate followers’ dynamic brand
experiences (O), which in turn evoke spontaneous purchase responses (R). This
pathway reflects how interactive and immersive digital environments can amplify the
emotional and cognitive mechanisms underlying impulsive consumption. Accordingly,
the following hypothesis is proposed:

120

H9: Dynamic brand experience positively influences followers’ impulse buying.

2.13 Mediating Roles of Dynamic Brand Experience
2.13.1 DBE as a Mediator between Streamers’ Attributes and Follower Engagement
Within the Stimulus –Organism–Response (SOR) framework, dynamic brand
experience represents the internal psychological state (organism) through which
external stimuli —such as streamers’ attributes of credibility, expertise, and
attractiveness —are transform ed into  follower engagement (response). Streamers
who effectively communicate trust, expertise, and relatability stimulate emotional and
cognitive reactions that enhance followers’ perception of the brand’s value and
interactivity, thereby strengthening their engagement with both the streamer an d the
brand (Brakus, Schmitt & Zarantonello, 2009; Yu et al., 2021; Wang et al., 2022).

This mediating role suggests that followers are not just responding to the streamer’s
traits directly, but rather to the experiential meaning that these attributes create. When
followers experience vivid, affective, and intellectually engaging brand intera ctions,
they feel more connected, valued, and participatory within the live streaming
environment. This heightened brand experience transforms passive viewers into
active participants, encouraging continuous engagement through commenting,
sharing, and emotional investment (Brodie et al., 2011; Valmohammadi et al., 2024).

From a theoretical perspective, this mediation mechanism demonstrates how
streamers’ attributes serve as triggers of experiential states that foster interactive
engagement. It underscores the importance of understanding engagement as a

121

response driven by emotional immersion and cognitive involvement rather than just
exposure to persuasive cues. Accordingly, the following hypothesis is proposed:

H10: Dynamic brand experience mediates the relationship between streamers’
attributes and followers’ engagement.

2.13.2 DBE as a Mediator between Streamers’ Attributes and Impulse Buying
In the context of live streaming commerce, dynamic brand experience serves as a
crucial psychological bridge linking streamers’ attributes —such as credibility,
expertise and attractiveness  to followers’ impulse buying behaviour. Within the
Stimulus–Organism–Response (SOR) framework, streamer attributes function as
external stimuli (S) that evoke internal experiential states (O), represented by dynamic
brand experience, which in turn lead to immediate purchase actions (R) (Mehrabian &
Russell, 1974; Brakus, Schmitt & Zarantonello, 2009).

Streamers who effectively display credibility, product expertise, and engaging
communication styles create immersive and emotional experiences that heighten
sensory and affective arousal. This dynamic brand experience reduces cognitive
resistance and promo tes spontaneous decision-making (Xu, Huang & Shang, 2021;
Khoi, Le & Nguyen Dong, 2023). The sensory richness of visuals, the immediacy of
feedback, and the emotional appeal of the streamer’s interaction combine to create a
compelling psychological environ ment where followers feel both entertained and
persuaded to act on impulse (Liu et al., 2023; Sarker & Dwivedi, 2024).

122

This mediation highlights that followers’ impulse purchases are not directly triggered
by the streamer’s traits alone, but by the experiential and emotional states that those
traits evoke. Dynamic brand experience thus acts as the mechanistic link that
translates perceived credibility and expertise into purchase behaviour by fostering
heightened emotional involvement, sensory stimulation, and cognitive absorption
during the live stream. Accordingly, the following hypothesis is proposed:

H11: Dynamic brand experience mediates the relationship between streamers’
attributes and followers’ impulse buying.

2.14 Serial Mediation Pathways
2.14.1 Streamers’ Attributes to Followers’ Engagement through Sense Presence, and
DBE
Building on the SOR framework, a sequential mediation pathway is proposed in which
streamers’ attributes (Stimulus, S) shape followers’ sense of presence, which then
enhances their dynamic brand experience (Organism, O), ultimately influencing
engagement behaviour (Response, R). This perspective recognises that engagement
in live streaming is rarely triggered by a single psychological process; instead, it
emerges through layered experiential mechanisms activated during real -time
interaction.

First, streamers’ attributes —such as credibility, authenticity, and expertise—are
known to heighten viewers’ sense of presence, including both social presence (feeling
socially connected) and telepresence (feeling immersed in the mediated environment)
(Ming et al., 2021; Ma, Zou & Lv, 2022; Tian & Frank, 2024). These presence -driven

123

psychological states help followers feel in the moment, intensifying their attentional
focus and emotional absorption.

Second, a heightened sense of presence creates the conditions for richer dynamic
brand experiences by deepening sensory immersion, increasing emotional resonance,
and strengthening cognitive engagement with the brand (Khoi, Le & Nguyen Dong,
2023; Sarker &  Dwivedi, 2024). When followers feel both socially connected and
perceptually immersed in a  live stream, they interpret the brand’s message more
vividly and develop stronger experiential associations.

Finally, richer dynamic brand experiences translate into greater follower engagement,
as followers respond with more likes, comments, participation in discussions, and
sustained attention during the live streaming (Gu, Cheng & Shen, 2023; Li et al., 2023).
This demonstrates that engagement is not just a behavioural reaction to streamer
attributes but is shaped by the experiential states that those attributes evoke.

Taken together, this sequential pathway highlights that followers become more
engaged when credible and authentic streamers first evoke a strong sense of
presence, which then amplifies the quality of the brand experience. Accordingly, the
following hypothesis is proposed:

H12: Streamers’ attributes indirectly influence followers’ engagement through sense
of presence and dynamic brand experience.

124

2.14.2 Streamers’ Attributes to Impulse Buying through Sense Presence, and DBE
Within the SOR framework, an indirect pathway is proposed in which streamers’
attributes (Stimulus, S) first evoke a heightened sense of presence, which
subsequently enhances the dynamic brand experience (Organism, O), ultimately
leading to impulse buying behaviour (Response, R). This pathway acknowledges that
impulsive purchases in live streaming environments are not random but are shaped
by layered psychological processes triggered during real-time interactions.

Streamers’ attributes —such as credibility, expertise, and attractiveness serve as
powerful stimuli that cultivate a strong sense of presence during live streaming
sessions. When viewers perceive streamers as credible and professional, the
immersive qualities of the stream are amplified. This produces intensified feelings of
social presence (the sense of real -time interpersonal connection) and telepresence
(the sensation of being immersed within the mediated environment) (Ming et al., 2021;
Tian & Frank, 2024). These presence-driven states reduce psychological distance and
heighten emotional involvement, making followers more receptive to persuasive cues.

A strong sense of presence then enhances followers’ dynamic brand experience,
enriching their sensory, affective, and behavioural engagement with the brand (Khoi,
Le & Nguyen Dong, 2023; Sarker & Dwivedi, 2024). When followers feel absorbed in
the stream, they process information more fluently, experience greater emotional
resonance, and perceive the brand message as more vivid and compelling.

These enriched experiences increase the likelihood of impulse buying behaviour, as
viewers respond to heightened sensory stimulation, emotional arousal, and moment -

125

to-moment engagement. Live streaming environments are particularly conducive to
impulsive purchases because presence -driven immersion reduces deliberation time,
intensifies affective responses, and increases perceptions of product desirability and
urgency (Xu, Huang & Shang, 2021; Liu et al., 2023).

Thus, the indirect mediation pathway reflects how streamers’ attributes shape viewers’
immediate psychological states (sense of presence), which subsequently elevate their
experiential evaluations (dynamic brand experience), leading to spontaneous
purchasing decisions. Accordingly, the following hypothesis is proposed:

H13: Streamers’ attributes indirectly influence followers’ impulse buying through sense
of presence and dynamic brand experience.

2.14.3 Streamers’ Attributes to Followers’ Engagement through Congruence and DBE
Within live streaming commerce, the pathway from streamers’ attributes to follower
engagement often unfolds through a sequence of psychological mechanisms rather
than a direct effect. Following Congruence Theory (Osgood & Tannenbaum, 1955;
Sirgy, 1982), when followers perceive that the streamer’s persona aligns with both the
brand identity and their own self-concept, they experience a sense of motivational and
value-based harmony. This congruence deepens followers’ emotional resonance with
the content and increases their receptiveness to the brand narrative presented during
the live stream (Venciute et al., 2023; Liang & Yoon, 2022; Zhang & Hu, 2024).

Once this sense of congruence is established, it strengthens the formation of a
dynamic brand experience —a multidimensional construct comprising sensory,

126

affective, intellectual, and behavioural elements. Dynamic brand experience functions
as the experiential bridge that converts psychological alignment into meaningful
engagement and impulse buying. Followers who perceive a coherent, authentic, and
immersive brand -streamer-follower relationship are more likely to interpret the live
stream as personally relevant, engaging, and rewarding, which naturally enhances
their willingness to interact, participate, and sustain engagement with the stream.

From the perspective of the SOR framework, streamers’ attributes serve as the
stimulus (S), congruence represents the cognitive-affective organismic response (O1),
and dynamic brand experience reflects a deepened experiential state (O2), together
driving t he final behavioural response of follower engagement (R). This sequential
pathway clarifies why individual streamer attributes alone are insufficient for long-term
engagement—followers require both psychological alignment and immersive
experiential reinforcement. Accordingly, the following hypothesis is proposed:

H14: Streamers’ attributes indirectly affect followers’ engagement through brand–
streamer–follower congruence and dynamic brand experience.

2.14.4 Streamers’ Attributes to Impulse Buying through Congruence and DBE
Impulse buying in live streaming commerce is strongly shaped by the psychological
coherence followers perceive between the streamer, the brand, and themselves.
According to Congruence Theory (Osgood & Tannenbaum, 1955; Sirgy, 1982), when
followers observe that a streamer’s credibility fits the brand’s identity and
simultaneously reflects their own values or aspirations, they are more likely to
internalise the streamer’s recommendations as trustworthy and self -relevant. This

127

alignment lowers cognitive resistance and increases the emotional receptivity that
often precedes spontaneous purchase decisions (Venciute et al., 2023; Zhang & Hu,
2024).

Once congruence is established, it enhances followers’ dynamic brand experience by
enriching their affective and sensory engagement with the product being presented.
Dynamic brand experience —involving heightened emotional arousal, vivid mental
imagery, and intensified cognitive involvement—creates the psychological conditions
under which impulse purchasing becomes more likely. Followers who feel both aligned
with the streamer and immersed in the brand experience are more susceptible to time-
limited promotio ns, persuasive demonstrations, and emotionally charged product
presentations typical of live streaming environments.

Within the SOR framework, streamers’ attributes act as the initial stimulus (S), while
perceived congruence forms the first organismic response (O1), priming followers to
process the live stream’s content more favourably. Dynamic brand experience then
operates as a second organismic layer (O2), amplifying emotional impulses and
reducing deliberative control—ultimately leading to spontaneous purchases (R). This
sequential mediation explains why impulse buying is unlikely to be triggered by
streamer attribute s alone; instead, it emerges from the combined influence of
psychological alignment and immersive brand experiences. Accordingly, the following
hypothesis is proposed:

H15: Streamers’ attributes indirectly influence followers’ impulse buying through
brand–streamer–follower congruence and dynamic brand experience.

128

2.15 Moderating Effects (Contextual Factors)
2.15.1 Gender
Although dynamic brand experience plays a central role in shaping followers’
behavioural responses, its influence may vary across demographic groups ,
particularly followers' gender. Prior research suggests that males and females often
differ in their emotional processing, risk perception, and responsiveness to digital
marketing stimuli (Chen et al., 2021; Liu et al., 2022). Female  followers, for example,
are generally more sensitive to affective cues and interpersonal warmth, whereas men
followers may respond more to informational clarity or performance-focused content.

In the context of live streaming commerce, dynamic brand experience encompasses
sensory richness, emotional resonance, and interactive engagement, which may be
interpreted differently depending on followers’ gender-based preferences or
motivations. As a result, the degree to which followers translate immersive brand
experiences into active engagement (e.g., participating in chat, liking, sharing, or
reacting to promotions) could differ across genders.

From an SOR perspective, followers’ gender acts as a contextual variable that shapes
how organismic states —such as emotional and cognitive responses generated by
dynamic brand experience—are transformed into followers’ engagement. Thus, based
on the existing literature, female followers are expected to respond more strongly to
dynamic brand experiences than male followers, resulting in higher levels of
engagement. Accordingly, the following hypothesis is proposed.

129

H16: Gender moderates the relationship between dynamic brand experience and
followers’ engagement, with the positive relationship expected to be stronger for
female followers than for male followers.

Also, Impulse buying in live streaming environments is strongly influenced by
emotional arousal, sensory stimulation, and the immediacy created by dynamic brand
experiences. However, existing research indicates that men and women often differ in
their susceptibility to these psychological triggers. Women tend to rely more heavily
on affective cues and experiential enjoyment when making spontaneous decisions,
whereas men may be more influenced by informational cues or perceived utility (Chen
et al., 2021; Kong, Zhan & Zhu, 2023).

Given these distinctions, the extent to which a vivid and immersive brand experience
translates into impulsive purchase behaviour is likely to vary by gender. In the SOR
framework, gender shapes how internal emotional states —generated by dynamic
brand experience—are converted into impulse buying. Thus, gender may amplify or
weaken the link between immersive brand experiences and the tendency to buy
impulsively in live streaming contexts. Accordingly, the following hypothesis is
proposed.

H17: Gender moderates the relationship between dynamic brand experience and
followers’ impulse buying.

130

2.15.2 Product type
Different product categories influence different levels of consumer involvement,
emotional responses, and information -processing styles. Hedonic products products
such as electronics, beauty devices, or luxury goods often require more detailed
evaluation, leading followers to engage more actively with the streamer and the brand
when the dynamic brand experience is strong. In contrast, utilitarian products—such
as household supplies or everyday consumables —may evoke more habitual or
convenience-driven responses, resulting in weaker engagement even when the
experiential quality is high (Guo, Zhang & Wang, 2022; Lei, Shi & Liu, 2023; Lu, Li &
Ge, 2023).

From the SOR perspective, product type acts as a contextual factor that influences
how followers interpret and respond to experiential stimuli. A rich and immersive brand
experience may significantly enhance engagement for visually rewarding or hedonic
products, while having a more limited effect for products with primarily functional
benefits. This suggests that the strength of the dynamic brand experience to the
follower engagement pathway may upon the nature of the product being promoted.
Accordingly, the following hypothesis is proposed.

H18: Product type  (hedonic vs. utilitarian ) moderates the relationship between
dynamic brand experience and followers’ engagement.

On the other hand, Impulse buying tendencies differ substantially across product
categories. Hedonic or visually appealing products—such as cosmetics, fashion items,
or lifestyle accessories—often stimulate spontaneous purchasing because they evoke

131

emotional gratification and sensory pleasure. When paired with a rich dynamic brand
experience, these products can trigger stronger impulsive responses, as followers
become more immersed, excited, and persuaded in the moment (Guo, Zhang & Wang,
2022; Lin, Gui & Lu, 2024; Li et al., 2025).

Conversely, products with primarily utilitarian benefits —such as home appliances,
health-related goods, or functional everyday items —typically require more rational
evaluation. Even when the dynamic brand experience is strong, followers may exhibit
lower impulse buying tendencies because these products are asso ciated with
practicality, long-term use, or higher financial risk.

Within the SOR framework, product type operates as a contextual moderator that
shapes how experiential stimuli translate into impulsive behavioural responses. Thus,
the extent to which dynamic brand experience drives impulse buying depends on
whether the p roduct category naturally aligns with hedonic, emotional purchasing
versus functional decision-making.

H19: Product type moderates the relationship between dynamic brand experience and
followers’ impulse buying behaviour.

2.16 Live Streaming Commerce Framework

Figure 4 below summarises the complete set of hypotheses developed in this chapter
and visually depicts the underlying logic of the proposed model. It illustrates how
streamer attributes function as key stimuli that activate psychological mechanisms in

132

the follower, namely sense of presence and brand –streamer–follower congruence,
which then contribute to the formation of dynamic brand experience. This experience
serves as the central pathway through which followers’ engagement and impulse
buying intentio ns emerge, while gender and product type moderate selected
behavioural relationships. Together, the diagram and accompanying hypotheses
capture the structural, mediating, and moderating relationships that define follower
behaviour in live streaming commerce. This integrated visual representation provides
a clear foundation for the empirical analysis presented in the next chapter.

133

Figure 4: Conceptual Model and Summary of Hypothesised Relationships

134

2.17 Summary
This chapter developed the hypotheses underpinning the conceptual framework of the
study by drawing on the Stimulus –Organism–Response (SOR) model and different
theories. Building on the literature reviewed in Chapter 2, the chapter clarified how
streamers’ attributes (stimulus) activate key psychological mechanisms (organism) —
sense of presence, congruence, and dynamic brand experience—that shape followers’
engagement and impulse buying behaviour (response).

The first set of hypotheses addressed the direct effects of streamers’ attributes on
sense of presence and congruence, establishing these psychological mechanisms as
core pathways through which followers interpret and respond to live-streaming content.
Then articulated how sense of presence and congruence independently and
significantly influence dynamic brand experience, reinforcing their roles as internal
evaluative states consistent with SOR-based organism constructs.

Subsequently, explained how dynamic brand experience functions as a central
experiential mechanism linking psychological states to engagement and impulse
buying. Hypotheses were developed to capture its direct effects on engagement and
impulse buying, as well as its mediating role between streamers’ attributes and
consumer engagement and Impulse Buying . Additional sequential mediation
hypotheses were proposed to examine multi-step pathways involving combinations of
presence, congruence, and dynamic brand experience.

135

Finally, moderating hypotheses were introduced to explore how contextual factors —
specifically gender and product type—shape the strength of the relationships between
dynamic brand experience and engagement and impulse buying behaviour . These
moderators allow the model to account for heterogeneity in audience reactions within
live-streaming environments.

Overall, this chapter systematically articulated the theoretical logic that connects all
variables in the proposed model. Organising hypotheses into coherent thematic
groups aligned with the SOR framework provides a clear foundation for the research
and analyses presented in the following chapters.

136

3 Chapter 4: Research Methodology
This chapter outlines the research methodology used in this study, providing a detailed
explanation and justification of the research design, data collection, and analysis
procedures. The objective is to ensure transparency, credibility, and alignment with
the research questions and objectives by clearly demonstrating how the chosen
methods are appropriate for investigating follower engagement and impulse buying in
live streaming commerce. Given the interactive nature of live streaming  commerce,
this study examines how streamer attributes—such as the sense of presence, brand-
streamer-follower congruence, and dynamic brand experiences —impact follower
engagement and impulse buying within live streaming commerce.

This chapter outlines the research design, the identification of the target population
and sampling strategy, the data collection procedures, and the analytical methods
used to interpret the findings. Particular emphasis  is placed on ensuring
methodological rigour, with a focus on the validity and reliability of the research. Ethical
considerations, including participant confidentiality, informed consent, and compliance
with regulations, are also addressed to demonstrate adherence to ethical research
practices.

Ultimately, this chapter introduces a clear  research method that ensures the
quantitative approach aligns with the research objectives and enables a structured
investigation of the effectiveness of live streaming as a digital marketing strategy. By
systematically detailing each aspect of the research  methodology process, this
chapter provides a solid foundation for the subsequent data analysis and interpretation
of findings.

137

3.1 Overview of Research Methods
The research methodology is anchored by the researcher's epistemological and
ontological positions, which inform the researcher's worldview and understanding of
reality and knowledge. These foundational philosophical beliefs inevitably shape the
overall approach to the research, including the chosen methods and techniques for
gathering and interpreting data (Saunders, Lewis & Thornhill, 2019) . Also, the
theoretical framework of a research methodology refers to the selected paradigms or
theories that guide the design and interpretation of the study. The choice of a
theoretical framework is strongly influenced by the nature of the research question and
the field of research. It acts as a lens through which the researcher views and makes
sense of the data collected, offering context and explanation to the phenomena under
investigation (Schindler, 2022).

Research methodology is a structured process that demands a rigorous and
systematic approach, which is akin to the model of the research onion: Figure 5 below
(Saunders, Lewis & Thornhill, 2019). This model visualises the methodology as layers
of an onion, each layer representing a distinct stage of the research process. As each
layer - or research stage - is peeled away or completed, it reveals the subsequent
stage to be addressed, thereby marking the end of the process. Importantly, this model
necessitates a procedural approach where researchers must sequentially navigate
through each stage without skipping any step to reach the next (Saunders, Lewis &
Thornhill, 2019) . The key elements of a research onion include philosophical
orientation, approaches, strategies, time horizons, and methods for data collection and
analysis. A researcher must carefully select suitable components from each of these

138

key elements to develop an exhaustive research methodology tailored to a specific
problem area (Schindler, 2022; Eden, Nielsen & Verbeke, 2020).

Figure 5: Research Onion
The "Research Onion" is a metaphor used in research methodology to describe the
stages through which a researcher must pass when designing and conducting the
research. It was introduced by Saunders, Lewis, and Thornhill (2019) and visually
represents the research process as the layers of an onion. From the outer layers to
the inner core, each layer of the onion signifies a step that needs to be addressed in
a sequential manner, akin to peeling an onion.

The outermost layer is the research philosophy, which represents the researcher's
beliefs and assumptions about the world. The next layer refers to the research
approach, which could be inductive or deductive. The third layer is concerned with the
research strategy. The fourth layer denotes the researcher's choice of time horizon.

139

The innermost layers pertain to data collection and analysis methods.  The Research
Onion model highlights the importance of considering each element of the research
process carefully and sequentially. It offers a systematic approach to designing the
research and ensuring it is rigorous, coherent, and valid. It is very effective for
management studies (Schindler, 2022; Saunders, Lewis & Thornhill, 2019) . The
subsequent section provides a detailed account of the research methodology
implemented in this live streaming commerce research and offering justifications for
each decision made.

In the context of this study on live streaming commerce, the research onion framework
is directly applied. The research philosophy adopted is positivism, aligning with the
objective, measurable nature of the constructs under investigation. The approach is
deductive, as the research tests specific hypotheses derived from established
theories, such as Source Credibility Theory, Congruence Theory, and Flow Theory.
The strategy involves a quantitative questionnaire design, suitable for analysing
relationships between multiple latent variables across two cultural contexts (UK and
China). The time horizon is cross-sectional, as data were collected at a single point in
time to assess current follower engagement and impulse buying  behaviour and
perceptions. Finally, the data collection and analysis methods include structured
questionnaires and statistical modelling using SPSS and SmartPLS. By mapping each
of these decisions onto the layers of the research onion, this study ensures
methodological coherence and rigour, while tailoring each step to the specific
requirements of the research questions and objectives.

140

3.2 Research Philosophy
Research philosophy refers to the set of beliefs that guide a  business and
management researcher's approach to understanding the world, which involves the
nature of reality (ontology), how knowledge is created and developed (epistemology),
and the values associated with that knowledge (axiology) (Bell, 2019). It provides the
philosophical background for the research methodology and influences the
researcher's choice of research methods. Research philosophy is fundamental to the
research process because it determines how the researcher collects, analyses, and
interprets data (Creswell, 2014) . It helps the researcher recognise which designs
would work and which ones would not (Creswell & Poth, 2018). In short, the research
philosophy could have a profound impact on the way we approach our research, how
we interpret the findings, and how we perceive the world around us (Saunders, Lewis,
& Thornhill, 2012). There are several different types of research philosophies, such as
positivism, interpretivism, pragmatism, realism, and others. (Saunders, Lewis &
Thornhill, 2019) Each of these is described below.

Positivism, often associated with quantitative research, suggests that the world exists
as an objective reality, independent of human perception or interpretation. It
emphasises hypothesis testing using structured and statistically analysed data to
uncover causal relationships (Bell, 2019).  Critical Realism, while acknowledging an
objective reality, asserts that our understanding of that reality is always mediated by
our perceptions and social conditions. It emphasizes the role of context and looks to
uncover mechanisms that may not be imm ediately observable but still contribute to
phenomena (Bhaskar, 2008) . Interpretivism argues that reality is not objective but
rather socially constructed and subjective, emphasising the subjective experiences,

141

perceptions, and interpretations of individuals. It is often associated with qualitative
research methods, like interviews and observations. (Saunders, Lewis & Thornhill,
2019). Postmodernism, on the other hand, challenges the existence of a single
objective or subjective reality. It stresses that realities are multiple and fragmented,
often a product of power relations, language, and discourse. It encourages critical
interrogation of assumptions and dominant narratives (Schindler, 2022). Pragmatism
in research philosophy represents a practical approach that prioritises the research
question over strict adherence to any one methodological stance. It is underpinned
that the nature of the research question should guide the selection of resear ch
methods, theories, and data sources, rather than being constrained by traditional
dichotomies such as qualitative vs. quantitative or empirical vs. theoretical.
Pragmatists argue that the value of research lies in its ability to address real -world
problems and produce meaningful, actionable outcomes. This philosophy encourages
researchers to use whatever methods, strategies, or empirical materials are most
suitable for answering their specific questions. (Morgan, 2014).

All in all, there are 5 research philosophies in the research onion map . Different
research philosophies have different advantages, but Positivism is adopted as the
research philosophy for this research because it is guided by several key principles
that align with a quantitative research approach through questionnaire. This decision
is driven by four main considerations below, reflective of positivism 's emphasis on
objective analysis and empirical evidence. It also reflects the fitting of the research
philosophy to this live streaming commerce research.

142

Firstly, positivism is grounded in the belief in an objective reality that could be observed
and measured independently of the individuals involved. This stance is particularly
advantageous in the context of live streaming commerce, where behaviours,
preferences, and interactions could be quantified to reveal patterns and relationships
(Morgan, 2014; Saunders, Lewis & Thornhill, 2019) . For example, follower
engagement and impulse buying in this research. By adopting a positivist approach,
the research aims to objectively assess the effectiveness of streamer's attributes,
sense of presence, brand streamer follower congruence, and dynami c brand
experiences. And from a perspective that transcends individual subjective
experiences, focusing instead on observable phenomena that can be systematically
analysed.

Secondly, positivism advocates for the use of quantitative methods as a means to
collect data and test hypotheses. This methodological orientation is well -suited to the
aims of this live streaming commerce research, which seeks to quantify the impact of
streamers' attributes on followers’ engagement and impulse buying. Through
structured questionnaire, the study employs statistical analysis to validate theoretical
hypotheses and draw findings and conclusions based on empirical data, thus adhering
to the positivist emphasis on quantification (Bell, 2019).

Thirdly, the emphasis of positivism on empirical evidence and logical reasoning
ensures that research findings are based on verifiable data rather than speculation or
subjective interpretation (Saunders, Lewis & Thornhill, 2019) . This approach aligns
with the objective of understanding live streaming commerce from a standpoint that
values reliability and validity. By grounding the research in positivist principles, the

143

study aims to produce findings that could inform evidence -based strategies for
practitioners in the field of digital marketing, offering insights that are directly applicable
and grounded in empirical observation.

Fourthly, positivism supports the application of existing theories to frame research
questions and guide the analysis of data. Unlike pragmatism, which promotes
methodological and theoretical pluralism, positivism encourages a focused
examination of phenomena through the lens of specific theoretical frameworks. In this
study, theories related to live streaming commerce and follower engagement and
impulse buying  behaviour form the foundation for developing hypotheses that are
tested through quantitative methods (Schindler, 2022). This theoretical rigour ensures
that the research not only contributes to the existing body of knowledge but also
adheres to the principles of logical positivism, which values coherence, clarity, and
predictive power.

So, the choice of positivism as the research philosophy underpinning this study reflects
a commitment to objective analysis, empirical evidence, and the testing of hypotheses
through quantitative methods. By relying on questionnaire to gather data, the research
embraces positivism’s foundational belief in the possibility of uncovering universal
truths about live streaming commerce and digital marketing, thereby offering valuable,
generalizable insights into this dynamic field (Johnson & Onwuegbuzie, 2004).

Furthermore, this study adopts an objectivist ontological stance, assuming that
consumer perceptions and behaviours in live streaming commerce exist
independently of the researcher and can therefore be objectively measured

144

(Saunders, Lewis, & Thornhill, 2019; Bell, 2019). Epistemologically, the study follows
the positivist view that knowledge is generated through empirical observation,
quantitative measurement, and statistical testing  (Creswell & Creswell, 2018;
Saunders et al., 2019) . These philosophical assumptions are consistent with the
deductive approach, questionnaire survey, and PLS -SEM analysis adopted in this
research.

3.3  Research Approaches
Research approaches are fundamental to any research as they dictate the overall
strategy and direction for conducting a study. They refer to the logical reasoning the
researcher uses to build a bridge between the theoretical aspects of the research,
such as concepts and hypotheses, and the empirical world of observation and data
(Norman Blaikie, 2019) . There are three research approaches , include deductive,
inductive, and abductive reasoning.

Deductive reasoning begins with a theory or a hypothesis, which is then tested through
data collection. The goal of deductive reasoning is to test an existing theory,
hypothesis, or model within a new context or with a new set of data. It primarily uses
quantitative methods, aiming to measure or identify associations between variables
and often seeks to generalise findings to a larger population (Bryman, 2015).

Abductive reasoning, on the other hand, involves generating a plausible explanation
for an observed phenomenon. It is a back -and-forth process of developing a theory,
seeing how it fits with the empirical evidence, and then revising it to better account for
the data. It neither starts with a clear theory like deduction, nor purely from data like

145

induction, but instead continually refines theory in response to empirical insights
(Saunders, Lewis & Thornhill, 2019).

Induction is a research approach that typically begins with the collection of empirical
data. Rather than starting with a pre -existing theory or hypothesis, as in the case of
deductive research, inductive reasoning allows for patterns, themes, and potentia l
theoretical frameworks to emerge from the data itself. Inductive research is often
exploratory, seeking to understand the complexities of a phenomenon within a
particular context. This approach is typically associated with qualitative research
methods su ch as interviews, observations, and case studies, which provide rich,
detailed data from which to draw insights and build theory. Inductive research is
particularly useful in areas where little prior theory exists, or where the researcher
seeks to develop a more grounded understanding of a particular issue (Saunders,
Lewis & Thornhill, 2019; Eden, Nielsen & Verbeke, 2020).

So, the deductive approach is appropriate for this research because this study
summarises and proposes hypotheses based on existing literature in the field of live
streaming commerce, builds a theoretical model, and then uses data collection and
analysis m ethods to test the relationship between streamers’ attributes, sense of
presence, brand streamer follower congruence, dynamic brand experiences and
follower engagement and impulse buying. Besides, the deductive method,
characterised by its focus on testing theory-driven hypotheses through empirical data,
aligns with the structured and hypothesis -testing nature of quantitative research
(Saunders, Lewis & Thornhill, 2019). So , deductive reasoning is well -suited for this

146

research as it enables the researcher to measure the relationships and influences
among different variables within the live streaming commerce context.

Quantitative research methods, such as questionnaires, are instrumental in gathering
numerical data that can be used to test the hypotheses formulated from the theoretical
framework. This approach ensures that the findings are based on observable,
empirical evidence that can be quantified and analysed stati stically (Tharenou, 2007;
Krishnaswamy, 2009) . In the case of live streaming commerce, the deductive
approach allows for a systematic investigation into how different variables impact
follower engagement and impulse buying behaviours.

Questionnaires, as a data collection tool, provide a consistent and reliable means of
capturing data from a large sample, which is essential for the generalisation of results.
The standardised format of questionnaires ensures that data collected across different
respondents are comparable, thereby enhancing the validity and reliability of the
findings. Additionally, the use of questionnaires facilitates the analysis of complex
relationships b etween multiple variables through various statistical techniques
(Bryman, 2015; Schindler, 2022).

Given the rapidly evolving nature of digital marketing and the dynamic environment of
live streaming, the deductive approach offers a structured way to test whether the
theoretical assumptions hold true in the current context. By using a deductive method,
this research aims to contribute to the existing body of knowledge by confirming,
challenging, or enhancing the theoretical propositions regarding live streaming
commerce.

147

Adopting this methodological stance also reflects the pragmatic aspects of research
design. Questionnaires could be distributed widely and efficiently, making them a
practical choice for researchers seeking to collect data from diverse and dispersed
populations. This is particularly pertinent for live streaming commerce research, where
participants are often geographically scattered, for example, in China and UK and let
different participants take part in an online data collection platform.

3.4 Research Method
Research methodology encompasses the systematic, theoretical analysis of the
methods and principles associated with a branch of knowledge. It refers to the overall
strategy that guides a researcher's choice of research methods and techniques,
including whether to use qualitative or quantitative methods or a combination of both,
known as mixed methods (Creswell, 2018).

The qualitative research approach is a method used to understand people's views,
experiences, attitudes, behaviour, and interactions (Anon, 2023). It generates non -
numerical data and focuses on capturing qualitative information in depth rather than
quantifying data and generali sing the results across a larger population. Qualitative
research often involves methods such as interviews, focus groups, and observations,
providing rich, contextual explorations of the topic at hand (Anon, 2023) . This
approach is rooted in interpretive philosophy , which views reality as subjective and
stresses the socially constructed nature of reality. It seeks to interpret and make sense
of phenomena in terms of the meanings people bring to them. Qualitative research
aim to gather an in-depth understanding of consumer behaviour and the reasons that
govern such behaviour (Anon, 2023).

148

Quantitative research is a systematic investigation that utili ses quantifiable data to
derive results regarding a chosen subject matter. This type of empirical research
approach relies heavily on the idea of quantification, which involves the conversion of
data into a numerical format (Creswell, 2014; Bryman, 2016). The process essentially
includes the measurement, collection, analysis, and interpretation of numerical data
to yield empirical evidence capable of demonstrating statistical relationships between
observed variables.

The quantitative research is embedded in positivist and post -positivist philosophy.
Positivists believe in the existence of a single truth, a stable and predictable reality
that can be understood objectively through observable facts and measurable
phenomena. Thus, quantitative research tends to have a structured, standardised, and
controlled approach to ensure the objectivity and reliability of the collected data
(Bryman, 2016). Quantitative research methods, such as questionnaires, experiments,
and statistical analysis of numerical data, are designed to gather data that can be
coded in a numeric form. These methods enable researchers to test specific
hypotheses by examining the relationship among variables. In this context, variab les
are often manipulated to discern cause-and-effect relationships, which further support
the verification or falsification of the proposed hypotheses (Bryman, 2016).

One of the primary advantages of quantitative research lies in its ability to provide
precise, quantifiable, and generalizable results. By relying on numerical and statistical
data, it allows for a broader study, involving a larger number of subjects, enha ncing
the generalizability of the results. Also, this methodology is reproducible, meaning that

149

the same study can be repeated under the same conditions to verify the results
(Creswell & Creswell, 2017).

The decision to use a single method, specifically questionnaires, for research on the
effectiveness of live streaming commerce is strategic, given the focused objectives of
this study. Utilising questionnaires as a quantitative research tool aligns with the need
for empirical data to test the formulated hypotheses regarding live streaming
commerce (Saunders, Lewis & Thornhill, 2019). While mixed methods research offers
the advantage of combining qualitative and quantitative insights, a single quantitative
method provides a streamlined, efficient pathway to understanding specific
phenomena through s tatistical analysis. Questionnaires are particularly effective for
gathering data on a wide range of variables from a large sample, making it possible to
generalise findings across the broader live streaming audience. This approach is
advantageous for quantifying the relationships between streamers' attributes, dynamic
brand experiences, and follower engagement and impulse buying  behaviour.
Quantitative methods, such as questionnaires, offer the precision and rigour needed
for testing theoretical propositions and deriving measurable insights (Bell, 2019).

Although qualitative methods provide depth and context to consumer behaviours and
interactions, the focus of this research on quantifiable aspects of live streaming
commerce, such as the degree of follower engagement or impulse buying ,
necessitates a methodological approach that could produce statistically valid results.
For example, the use of questionnaires allows for the collection of data that could be
analysed to reveal patterns, trends, and correlations within the live streaming
commerce domain. Using questionnaires as the sole method also addresses practical

150

considerations, such as resource constraints and the feasibility of reaching dispersed
digital followers. This approach enables the efficient collection of data from participants
across different geographies , for example UK and China, who engage with live
streaming platforms, providing a broad basis for analysis.

All in all, while the mixed methods approach has its merits in offering a comprehensive
view by marrying qualitative depth with quantitative breadth, the choice of a single
quantitative method through questionnaires is purposefully selected for this research.
Because it has more generalisability, statistical robustness and objectivity, at the same
time, live streaming commerce research needs objective measurement in evaluating
efficacy. It serves the aim of systematically examining the efficacy of live streaming
commerce within a structured, empirical framework, focusing on measurable
outcomes that contribute to the academic understanding of digital marketing strategies
in the context of live streaming commerce.

3.5  Research Strategies
The research strategy is an organised plan that guides the researcher in effectively
answering the research question or solving the research problem. This plan lays out
the overall approach and detailed methods that is used to collect, analyse, and present
data. It is guided by the research objectives, the nature of the topic, and the specific
constraints of the research context (Saunders, Lewis & Thornhill, 2019) . The choice
of research strategy determine s how the research is conducted, what kind of data is
gathered, how that data is analysed, and how the findings is interpreted. Also,
strategies could include questionnaires, case studies, experiments, ethnography,
grounded theory, and action research. The selection of a particular strategy depends

151

on several factors, including the research questions, the researcher's philosophical
commitments, and practical considerations such as time and resources (Bryman,
2016).

Questionnaires, and in particular questionnaires, are a widely used research strategy
that allows for the collection of data from a large sample size. They are an efficient
and cost -effective way to gather data on people's beliefs, experiences, and
behaviours. Questionnaires can be structured to provide quantitative data that can be
statistically analysed to identify trends, correlations, and patterns, thus providing
evidence of the effectiveness of live streaming commerce (Creswell, 2018) .
Furthermore, the standardi sation inherent in questionnaire design ensures that all
participants are asked the same questions, lending to the reliability and replicability of
the research.

So, the questionnaire in this research is an example of the quantitative questionnaire-
based approac h. This methodological choice takes advantage of the strengths of
quantitative (questionnaire) strategies. For the research on the effectiveness of live
streaming commerce, this combination offers an effective approach. Questionnaires
could provide broad, generalisable data about live streaming commerce effectiveness.
The result of the data enhances the validity and richness of findings, making
questionnaires an optimal choice for the research (Brannen, 2016).

3.6 Research Design
There are many different approaches to research design, and questionnaire research
is one of them. The questionnaire is a quantitative research instrument commonly

152

used to collect data from a predefined group of respondents to gain information and
insights on various topics of interest. It typically involves asking people for information
through a structured format of questions that participants respond to. Questionnaires
can be conducted in various ways, such as online or over the phone. Also, the 7-point
Likert scale has been used during the questionnaire. The Likert scale is a rating scale
often used in questionnaires to measure how people feel about an issue. A 7 -point
Likert scale, for instance, includes options such as "strongly disagree," "so mewhat
disagree" "disagree," "neither agree nor disagree," "agree," and "somewhat agree"
"strongly agree." Respondents indicate their level of agreement with a statement
related to the topic being questioned. The use of a 7 -point Likert scale allows
respondents to express their feelings  about live streaming commerce, providing a
range of options and opinions that can result in more precise data. This type of scale
is commonly used because it's simple for participants to understand, and it's efficient
for researchers to analyse (Bryman, 2016).

3.7 Measurement
The questionnaire instrument was developed based on established, validated scales
from previous studies. The items measuring key constructs —such as follower
engagement, steamers’ attributes, and live streaming effectiveness —were adapted
from existing literature to ensure content validity  for example:  Attractiveness (Li &
Peng, 2021b; Zheng et al., 2023; Zhou & Huang, 2023) , Authenticity (Li & Peng,
2021b; He, Li & Xue, 2022), Credibility (Lin, 2021; Ma, Zou & Lv, 2022), Expertise (Li
& Peng, 2021; Zheng et al., 2023; Guo, Zhang & Wang, 2022),  Telepresence (Li &
Peng, 2021b; Sun et al., 2019b) , Social Presence (Lin, 2021; Ma, Zou & Lv, 2022;
Zhang, Cheng & Huang, 2023; Sun et al., 2019b) , Congruence (Wei et al., 2022) ,

153

Dynamic Brand Experience ( Yu et al., 2021) , Engagement  (Wongkitrungrueng &
Assarut, 2020a; Yu & Zheng, 2021) , and Impulse Buying (Fu & Hsu, 2023; Zhang,
Cheng & Huang, 2023). Also, some modifications were made to the wording of these
items to better align them with the context of live marketing commerce while
maintaining their original meaning. For example, the streamers’ attributes  are split
from the previous questionnaire into three different sub-factors: expertise, credibility,
and credibility. Then the streamers’ attributes are split from the previous questionnaire
into three different sub -factors: expertise, credibility, authenticity, and so on. This
allows participants to better understand the questionnaire content.

To ensure the reliability and validity of the instrument, a pilot study was conducted with
a small sample before full -scale data collection. The pilot test assessed the clarity of
the questions  about customers’ experiences in live streaming commerce  and the
effectiveness of the scale, and any potential ambiguities. In detail, there  are 100
participants be invited who have watched or participated in live streaming commerce
before to fill out a questionnaire to ensure that all of the questionnaire questions were
related to the field of live streaming commerce. The participants came from different
backgrounds, like people living in China and UK, then ages ranged from 18 to 60. They
also had different live streaming engaging or shopping experiences, which were highly
relevant to our formal participant profile. Based on the participant s’ feedback, minor
refinements were made to enhance the questionnaire’s comprehensibility and
applicability to the research context. For example, 1. In the pilot test version, I did not
explain the terms in live streaming, which caused some participants to get confused,
so I explained each term after receiving feedback. 2. Some participants were not clear

154

about what the live streaming commerce feature included, so a short introduction video
is added at the beginning.

By employing well-established scales with appropriate modifications and validating the
instrument through a pilot study, for example, I further subdivided the questions about
Attributes into 5 different direction s and Congruence into 3 different directions . This
research ensures robust data collection, facilitating a comprehensive analysis of
follower engagement and impulse buying in live streaming commerce. In the end the
measurement scales adopted for each construct are summarised in Appendix 7, while
the full questionnaire is presented in Appendix 2.

3.8  Data Collection
The data collection procedure is a key element for this  research, ensuring the
acquisition of relevant and reliable data to address the research
objectives(Krishnaswamy, 2009; Bell, 2019) . For this study, an online questionnaire
was employed to collect quantitative data from participants in both China and the UK.
This dual -country approach allows for cross -cultural comparisons and provides a
broader perspective on live streaming commerce. The questionnaire was distributed
electronically using secure questionnaire platforms - Qualtrics, ensuring accessibility
of participation (Verma, 2020) . The population of interest comprised consumers in
China and the United Kingdom who had previous experience watching or purchasing
through live streaming commerce platforms. A purposive sampling strategy was
adopted to recruit participants who were familiar  with live streaming commerce and
therefore able to provide informed responses relevant to the research objectives.

155

A total of 750 questionnaires were distributed, resulting in 600 valid responses (300
from China and 300 from the UK). The final sample size was considered adequate for
PLS-SEM and cross-cultural comparison, providing sufficient statistical power for the
proposed analyses (Yockey, 2016; Stehlik-Barry & Babinec, 2017).

For people recruitment , participants were recruited through two primary channels:
Prolific for the United Kingdom sample and Wenjuanxing  (WJX) for the Chinese
sample. Prolific is a widely used online research platform known for providing high -
quality, demographically diverse participants for academic research, while WJX is one
of the most reputable and commonly used questionnaire platforms in China. The
questionnaire was hosted on Qualtrics, which ensured consistency in presentation and
data collection across both samples. All participants were informed of the academic
purpose of the study and provided informed consent before proceeding. Participation
was voluntary, a nd modest financial compensation was provided in line with each
platform’s standard remuneration policies to ensure engagement and data quality.

The 7-point Likert scale allows respondents to express varying levels of agreement to
disagreement with the statements, providing more  accurate data on follower
engagement and impulse buying in live marketing commerce. The sample size of 600
respondents is deemed statistically sufficient to enhance the reliability and
generalizability of the findings, ensuring robust statistical power in data ana lysis
(Yockey, 2016; Stehlik -Barry & Babinec, 2017) . The inclusion of respondents from
both China and the UK enhances the study’s validity, allowing for meaningful cross -
market insights.

156

Also, this study utilised the purposive sampling approach to specifically target
individuals who actively engage with streamers through live streaming platforms. This
method ensures that the collected data is highly relevant to the research objectives
(Krishnaswamy, 2009; Schindler, 2022) . Additionally, a diverse demographic
representation was sought to ensure variability in participant characteristics ; for
instance, I recruit participants from  different ages, genders, and different education
levels. Regarding the demographic result, this study achieved balance in responses
across different demographic groups to avoid bias and enhance the applicability of the
findings to broader followers.

To maintain ethical standards and ensure data protection, participants were informed
of their rights and provided with a consent form before completing the questionnaire.
Participation was entirely voluntary, and respondents had the right to withdraw at any
stage. Data confidentiality was strictly maintained, with all responses anonymised and
stored securely to comply with data protection regulations. By employing a rigorous
data collection strategy, ensuring representative sampling, and maintaining ethical
research practices, this study ensures the validity and reliability of the collected data,
contributing to meaningful insights into follower engagement and impulse buying in
live streaming commerce.

3.9 Data Analysis
Overall, all quantitative data were processed and analysed according to the following
steps. For the quantitative data obtained through the online questionnaires (Qualtrics),
SPSS (Statistical Package for the Social Sciences) and Smart PLS were used. PLS-
SEM was selected because this study aims to examine a complex conceptual model

157

involving multiple latent constructs, mediation effects, moderation effects, and cross -
cultural comparisons. In addition, PLS -SEM is particularly suitable for prediction -
oriented research, theory development, and analysing complex models without
requiring strict data normality assumptions (Hair et al., 2022). Therefore, SmartPLS
was considered the most appropriate analytical approach for addressing the research
objectives. Also, SPSS is a leading software for statistical analysis, well -suited to
