Appendix 2: Questionnaire
1.Streamers’ Attributes
1.1 Attractiveness
I feel that the live streamer is：
• Classy
• Beautiful
• Elegant
• Sexy
1.2 Trustworthiness
I think the streamer is.
• Dependable
• Honest
• Sincere
• Trustworthy
1.3 Expertise
I feel that the live streamer is:
• An expert
• Knowledgeable
• Qualified
• Experienced

325
2 Sense of Presence
2.1 Telepresence
When I watch live streaming, I feel:
• My body was in the room, but I felt my mind was inside the world created by
these streamers.
• I was immersed in the world these streamers had created.
• This live streaming generated world seemed to me to be “somewhere I visited”
rather than “something I see.”
• After watching the live stream, I feel like I come back to the “real world” after a
journey.
2.2 Social Presence
There is a sense of
• Human contact in live streaming.
• Personalness in live streaming.
• Human sensitivity in live streaming shopping.
• Human warmth in live streaming shopping.
3. Congruence
3.1 Streamer-Follower Congruence
• Concerning our characters, this streamer and I are very similar.
• I resemble the typical follower of this streamer’s channel very much.
• I can easily identify with this streamer.
• I resemble the typical follower of this channel very much.
• My ideals are very similar to the characteristics of this streamer.

326
• By participating in this live streaming, I want to show the best of me.
• My actual self-image is consistent with the overall image of this online celebrity.
• This streamer reflects my ideal self.
3.2 Streamer- Brand Congruence
I think：
• There’s a good fit between my favourite streamer and the brand he or she
endorses.
• My favourite streamer is compatible with the brand he or she endorses.
• My favourite streamer is congruent to the brand he or she endorses.
• It makes sense that my favourite streamer endorses the brand.
3.3 Follower – Brand Congruence
• The brands advertised through social media live streams are consistent with
how I see myself.
• The brands advertised through social media live streams reflect who I am.
• The brands advertised through social media live streams cater to people like
me.
• The typical customers of brands advertised through social media live streams
are like me.
• I think the consistency between me and the brand.
• I could use this brand to communicate who I am to other people.
• I think this brand could help me become the type of person I want to be.
4. Dynamic Brand Experience
4.1 Sensory:

327
• This brand makes a strong impression on my visual sense or other senses.
• The brand always appeals to me sensually.
• I find this brand interesting in a sensory way.
• I will be interested when I see, hear or come into contact with the brand on the
live streaming platform.
4.2 Affective:
This brand
• Induces feelings and sentiments.
• Is an emotional brand.
4.3 Intellectual:
• Engagement with this platform sparked my intellectual thinking.
• The brand makes me think.
• This brand stimulates my curiosity.
4.3 Behavioural:
• I engage in interactions with the brand.
• I find the brands encourage interactions on the brand.
5.Follower Engagement
In the live streaming, I would like to.
• Spend more time participating in the live streaming.
• Consider a streamer that uses live streaming platforms as my first choice when
I want to buy something.
• I recommend live streaming streamer to my friends.
• Become a fan and follow this streamer.

328
• Try and keep track of the activities of a streamer’s live streaming channel.
•  Revisit the streamer's page to watch their new live stream in the near future.
6.Impulse Buying
• While watching live streaming, I often buy things spontaneously.
• As I watch live streaming, I often buy things online without thinking.
• Watching live streaming, sometimes I am a bit reckless about what I buy.
• While watching the live streaming, I feel like buying more things than I need.

Appendix 6: Summary of Main Theories
Theory Key Authors Core Concepts Contribution
Source
Credibility
Theory
Hovland et al.
(1953); Ohanian
(1990); Gao et
al., 2021

Communicator effectiveness depends on
expertise, trustworthiness, and attractiveness.
Explains why streamer attributes matter as
persuasive signals. Unlike SOR, which explains
psychological processing, SCT explains the
quality of the stimulus itself. Provides justification
for why credibility -based streamer traits lead to
stronger presence/congruence and richer brand
experiences.
Congruence
Theory
Osgood &
Tannenbaum
(1955); Sirgy
(1982); (Liu,
Zhang & Zhang,
2020; Chen,
Behaviour and attitudes depend on perceived
alignment between self -image and external
objects (brand/ streamer).
Explains why fit between streamer –brand–
follower identities strengthen dynamic brand
experiences. Adds the relational and identity -
based mechanism missing in SOR and SCT.
Highlights psychological alignment rather than
persuasion or environmental stimuli.

337
Pham & Tran,
2024
Flow Theory Csikszentmihalyi
(1990); Liao et
al., 2022; Li et
al., 2023;
Immersive, focused attention states enhance
engagement and experiential processing.
Provides a mechanism for how sense of
presence enhances dynamic brand experience
by creating cognitive absorption. Unlike SCT or
congruence theory, Flow Theory focuses on
immersive experiential states, bridging presence
with emotional/cognitive brand engagement.
Customer
Engagement
Theory
Hollebeek et al.,
Citation 2019;
Rasool et al.,
Citation 2020
Customer engagement is a psychological
state resulting from interactive, co -creative
customer experiences with a brand,
characterised by cognitive, emotional, and
behavioural dimensions. Engagement
emerges through ongoing interactions rather
than passive consumption.
Supports the conceptualisation of follower
engagement as an outcome shaped by dynamic,
interactive live -stream environments. Explains
how streamer attributes and dynamic brand
experience stimulate followers’ cognitive
attention, emotional involvement, and
participation behaviours, ultimately driving
engagement and impulse buying.

338
Appendix 7: Summary of Constructs and Definition
Construct Definition Key References
Streamers’
Attributes
The perceived qualities of the streamer —such as, expertise, attractiveness, and
trustworthiness —that influence followers’ evaluation of the brand and the content.
Ohanian,1990; Li &
Peng, 2021; Zheng et
al., 2023; Zhou &
Huang, 2023
Sense of Presence The psychological perception of “being there” during the live stream ing,
comprising social presence (feeling socially connected) and telepresence (feeling
immersed in the mediated environment)
(Lin, 2021; Ma, Zou &
Lv, 2022; Zhang, Cheng
& Huang, 2023; Sun et
al., 2019b)
Congruence
(Streamer–Brand–
Follower
Congruence)
The perceived fit or alignment among the streamer’s persona, the brand identity,
and the follower’s self -concept, which shapes trust, emotional resonance, and
brand evaluations.

(Wei et al., 2022)

339
Dynamic Brand
Experience
The real -time, sensory, affective, behavioural, and intellectual, responses
followers have toward a brand during live streaming, influenced by interactivity
and presentation.
Yu et al., 2021
Follower
Engagement
Followers’ behavioural and emotional involvement during live streaming, including
attention, interaction, participation, and intention to continue engaging.
Wongkitrungrueng &
Assarut, 2020; Yu &
Zheng, 2021
Impulse Buying  A spontaneous, unplanned purchase decision triggered by emotional,
experiential, or contextual cues during live streaming interactions.
Fu & Hsu, 2023; Zhang,
Cheng & Huang, 2023
Gender  A demographic characteristic used to test whether males and females differ in
how dynamic brand experience translates into engagement or impulse buying.
Chen, H. et al. (2021),
He, Y., Li, W. and Xue,
J. (2022)
Product Type The category or nature of the product (e.g., hedonic vs. utilitarian), used to
examine whether product characteristics influence the strength of relationships.
Luo et al., 2024
